(2.3) reduce to\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \tilde{u}^{\alpha }\partial _\alpha \ln {\tilde{\rho }}+\partial _\alpha \tilde{u}^{\alpha }+^{(4)}\tilde{\Gamma }^{\alpha }_{\alpha \nu } \tilde{u}^\nu&=0,\qquad \tilde{u}^{\alpha }\partial _{\alpha } \tilde{u}^{\mu }+\tilde{u}^\alpha {^{(4)}\tilde{\Gamma }^{\mu }_{\alpha \nu } \tilde{u}^\nu } =0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}u~α∂αlnρ~+∂αu~α+(4)Γ~αναu~ν=0,u~α∂αu~μ+u~α(4)Γ~ανμu~ν=0.

The Rescaled Einstein–Dust System in CMCSH Gauge

Following the work of Andersson and Moncrief [2, 3], we hereon impose the CMCSH gauge which foliates by surfaces of constant mean curvature, taking advantage of the fact that on the Milne background \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau = \tilde{g}^{ab}\tilde{k}_{ab} = -3/t_c$$\end{document}τ=g~abk~ab=-3/tc.

Definition 2.1

(CMCSH gauge)\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} t=\tau ,\qquad H^a:=\tilde{g}^{cb}(\Gamma [\tilde{g}]^{a}_{cb}-\Gamma [\gamma ]^a_{cb})&=0. \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}t=τ,Ha:=g~cb(Γ[g~]cba-Γ[γ]cba)=0.

We next rescale our variables with respect to the mean curvature \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau $$\end{document}τ.

Definition 2.2

(Rescaled variables \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(g_{ab}, N, X^a, \Sigma _{ab}, u^a, u^0, \rho , \widehat{N}, \hat{u}^0)$$\end{document}(gab,N,Xa,Σab,ua,u0,ρ,N^,u^0) and logarithmic time T) The rescaled geometric variables are defined as 2.5a\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{array}{rlrl} g_{ab}& :=\tau ^2\tilde{g}_{ab},& g^{ab}:=& (\tau ^2)^{-1}\tilde{g}^{ab},\\ N& :=\tau ^2\tilde{N}, & X^a:=& \tau \tilde{X}^a,\\ \Sigma _{ab}& :=\tau {\tilde{\Sigma }}_{ab}. \end{array} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}gab:=τ2g~ab,gab:=(τ2)-1g~ab,N:=τ2N~,Xa:=τX~a,Σab:=τΣ~ab.Note \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{X}_a = \tilde{g}_{ab}\tilde{X}^b = \tau ^{-3} X_a$$\end{document}X~a=g~abX~b=τ-3Xa. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{u}^0:=\tilde{u}^\tau $$\end{document}u~0:=u~τ. The rescaled matter variables are defined as2.5b\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} u^a:=\tau ^{-2}\tilde{u}^a , \qquad \rho :=|\tau |^{-3}{\tilde{\rho }}, \qquad u^0 :=\tau ^{-2} \tilde{u}^0 . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ua:=τ-2u~a,ρ:=|τ|-3ρ~,u0:=τ-2u~0. Denote \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\widehat{N}}:=\frac{N}{3}-1$$\end{document}N^:=N3-1 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\hat{u}^0:=u^0-1/3$$\end{document}u^0:=u0-1/3. Finally we define the logarithmic time\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} T:=-\ln (\tau /(e\tau _0)), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}T:=-ln(τ/(eτ0)),which satisfies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _T=-\tau \partial _\tau $$\end{document}∂T=-τ∂τ.

The above definition means we have the following ranges \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau _0 \le \tau \nearrow 0$$\end{document}τ0≤τ↗0 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1 \le T \nearrow \infty $$\end{document}1≤T↗∞ where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau \nearrow 0$$\end{document}τ↗0 corresponds to the direction of cosmological expansion (i.e. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t_c \nearrow \infty $$\end{document}tc↗∞).

Lemma 2.3

The normalisation condition (2.2) implies\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} u^0&= \frac{1}{(N^2 - X_a X^a)}\Big (\tau X_a u^a+ \Big [\tau ^{2}(X_a u^a)^2 + (N^2 - X_aX^a)(\tau ^{2}g_{ab} u^a u^b+1)\Big ]^{1/2}\Big ) .\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}u0=1(N2-XaXa)(τXaua+[τ2(Xaua)2+(N2-XaXa)(τ2gabuaub+1)]1/2).

Proof

Using (2.2) we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 0 = (-\tilde{N}^2 + \tilde{X}_a\tilde{X}^a)(\tilde{u}^0)^2 + 2\tilde{X}_a \tilde{u}^a \tilde{u}^0 + \tilde{g}_{ab}\tilde{u}^a\tilde{u}^b+1. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}0=(-N~2+X~aX~a)(u~0)2+2X~au~au~0+g~abu~au~b+1.This is a quadratic equation in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{u}^0$$\end{document}u~0. The roots are\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \tilde{u}^0 = \frac{1}{2(-\tilde{N}^2 + \tilde{X}_a\tilde{X}^a)}\Big ( -2\tilde{X}_a \tilde{u}^a\pm \Big [4(\tilde{X}_a \tilde{u}^a)^2 - 4(-\tilde{N}^2 + \tilde{X}_a\tilde{X}^a)(\tilde{g}_{ab}\tilde{u}^a\tilde{u}^b+1)\Big ]^{1/2}\Big ) . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}u~0=12(-N~2+X~aX~a)(-2X~au~a±[4(X~au~a)2-4(-N~2+X~aX~a)(g~abu~au~b+1)]1/2).Applying the rescalings from Definition 2.2 we find\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \tilde{u}^0 = \frac{\tau ^4}{(N^2 - X_a X^a)}\Big (\! \tau ^{-1}X_a u^a\mp \Big [\!\tau ^{-2}(X_a u^a)^2 + (N^2 - X_aX^a)(\tau ^{-2}g_{ab} u^a u^b+\tau ^{-4})\!\Big ]^{1/2}\!\Big ) . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}u~0=τ4(N2-XaXa)(τ-1Xaua∓[τ-2(Xaua)2+(N2-XaXa)(τ-2gabuaub+τ-4)]1/2).Hence, we introduce the rescaled quantity \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^0 = \tau ^{-2} \tilde{u}^0 $$\end{document}u0=τ-2u~0 for the larger root. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nabla , \hat{\nabla }$$\end{document}∇,∇^ denote the Levi-Civita connection of the Riemannian metrics \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g, \gamma $$\end{document}g,γ respectively. The Christoffel symbols of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{g}$$\end{document}g¯ now become (see e.g. [1])\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} ^{(4)}\tilde{\Gamma }^{a}_{b c}&=\Gamma ^{a}_{b c}[g]-\Gamma _{bc}X^a,\quad&^{(4)}\tilde{\Gamma }^{a}_{0 0}&=\tau ^{-2}\Gamma ^a,\\ ^{(4)}\tilde{\Gamma }^{a}_{0 b}&=\tau ^{-1}\left( -\delta _b^a+\Gamma _b^a\right) ,\quad&^{(4)}\tilde{\Gamma }^{0}_{0 0}&=\tau ^{-1}(-2+\Gamma _R),\\ ^{(4)}\tilde{\Gamma }^{0}_{a b}&=\tau \Gamma _{ab},\quad&^{(4)}\tilde{\Gamma }^{0}_{0 a}&=\Gamma _a,\\ \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(4)Γ~bca=Γbca[g]-ΓbcXa,(4)Γ~00a=τ-2Γa,(4)Γ~0ba=τ-1-δba+Γba,(4)Γ~000=τ-1(-2+ΓR),(4)Γ~ab0=τΓab,(4)Γ~0a0=Γa,where we have introduced the following rescaled geometric components.

Definition 2.4

(Rescaled Christoffel components \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Gamma ^a, \Gamma ^a_b, \Gamma _R, \Gamma _{ab}, \overset{\circ }{\Gamma } ^a$$\end{document}Γa,Γba,ΓR,Γab,Γ∘a)\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \Gamma ^a&:=-\partial _T X^a-X^a-2\widehat{N} X^a+X^b\nabla _b X^a-2 N \Sigma _c^a X^c+{N}{\nabla }^a{N}\\&\quad \,+ \Big (N^{-1}\partial _T N-N^{-1} X^b\nabla _b N+N^{-1}\left( \Sigma _{bc}+\tfrac{1}{3}g_{bc}\right) X^b X^c\Big )X^a, \\ \Gamma ^a_b&:=-N\Sigma ^a_b-\delta _b^a\widehat{N}+\nabla _b X^a- N^{-1} X^a\nabla _b N+ N^{-1}\left( \Sigma _{bc}+\tfrac{1}{3}g_{bc}\right) X^c X^a, \\ \Gamma _R&:=N^{-1}\big (-\partial _T N+X^a\nabla _a N-(\Sigma _{ab}+\tfrac{1}{3}g_{ab})X^aX^b\big ), \quad \overset{\circ }{\Gamma } ^a :=\Gamma ^a - N \nabla ^a N, \\ \Gamma _{ab}&=-N^{-1}(\Sigma _{ab}+\frac{1}{3}g_{ab}),\qquad \Gamma _a:=N^{-1}(\nabla _a N-(\Sigma _{ab}+\tfrac{1}{3}g_{ab})X^b).\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Γa:=-∂TXa-Xa-2N^Xa+Xb∇bXa-2NΣcaXc+N∇aN+(N-1∂TN-N-1Xb∇bN+N-1Σbc+13gbcXbXc)Xa,Γba:=-NΣba-δbaN^+∇bXa-N-1Xa∇bN+N-1Σbc+13gbcXcXa,ΓR:=N-1(-∂TN+Xa∇aN-(Σab+13gab)XaXb),Γ∘a:=Γa-N∇aN,Γab=-N-1(Σab+13gab),Γa:=N-1(∇aN-(Σab+13gab)Xb).

Remark 2.5

(Background solutions) The rescaled background (B) Milne geometry written in CMCSH gauge is\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} (g_{ab}, \Sigma _{ab}, N, X^a)|_B\equiv (\gamma , 0, 3, 0). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(gab,Σab,N,Xa)|B≡(γ,0,3,0).Furthermore,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} (\Gamma ^a, \Gamma ^a_b, \Gamma _R, \Gamma _a) |_B \equiv 0, \quad \Gamma _{ab} |_B \equiv -\tfrac{1}{9} \gamma _{ab}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(Γa,Γba,ΓR,Γa)|B≡0,Γab|B≡-19γab.Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho _0'>0$$\end{document}ρ0′>0 be a constant. The background, uniformly quiet fluid solution in CMCSH gauge is\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} (u^0, u^i, \rho )|_B = (\tfrac{1}{3},0,\rho _0'); \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(u0,ui,ρ)|B=(13,0,ρ0′);see also Appendix A.

We next evaluate certain energy momentum and matter source terms arising from the dust.

Definition 2.6

(Matter source terms \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E, \jmath ^a, \eta , S_{ab}, \underline{T}^{ab}$$\end{document}E,ȷa,η,Sab,T_ab)\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} E&:=\rho (u^0)^2 N^2,\quad \jmath ^a :=\rho N u^0 u^a, \\ \eta&:=E+ \rho g_{ab}(u^0 X^a+ \tau u^a)(u^0 X^b + \tau u^b), \\ S_{ab}&:=\rho ( u^0 X_a+ \tau u_a )(u^0 X_b+ \tau u_b) + \tfrac{1}{2}\rho g_{ab}, \quad \underline{T}^{ab} :=\rho u^a u^b. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}E:=ρ(u0)2N2,ȷa:=ρNu0ua,η:=E+ρgab(u0Xa+τua)(u0Xb+τub),Sab:=ρ(u0Xa+τua)(u0Xb+τub)+12ρgab,T_ab:=ρuaub.For further details on these definitions see Appendix 11.

Definition 2.7

(Matter source terms \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F_{u^j}, F_{u^0}, F_\rho $$\end{document}Fuj,Fu0,Fρ) 2.6\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} F_{u^j}&:=\tau ^{-1}(u^0)^2\overset{\circ }{\Gamma } ^j + ({\Gamma ^{j}_{k l}}[g]-{\Gamma ^{j}_{k l}}[\gamma ])u^ku^l+2u^0u^i\Gamma ^j_i+\tau u^k u^i \Gamma _{ki}X^j, \\ F_{u^0}&:=(u^0)^2\Gamma _R+2\tau u^ju^0\Gamma _j + \tau ^2 u^k u^j \Gamma _{kj}, \\ F_\rho&:=\tau \nabla _i u^i + \Gamma ^i_i u^0 - \tau \Gamma _j u^j + \tau \Gamma _{ik}X^i u^k - \tau \frac{u^j}{u^0} \nabla _j u^0 -\tau ^2\frac{ u^k u^j}{u^0} \Gamma _{kj}.\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Fuj:=τ-1(u0)2Γ∘j+(Γklj[g]-Γklj[γ])ukul+2u0uiΓij+τukuiΓkiXj,Fu0:=(u0)2ΓR+2τuju0Γj+τ2ukujΓkj,Fρ:=τ∇iui+Γiiu0-τΓjuj+τΓikXiuk-τuju0∇ju0-τ2ukuju0Γkj.

Equations of Motion

Bringing together all the previous notation, as well as using the general equations presented in [1], the equations of motion for the Einstein-Dust system in CMCSH gauge are the following. We have two constraint equations. 2.7a\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \text {R}(g)-|\Sigma |_g^2+\tfrac{2}{3}&= 4 \tau E, \\ \nabla ^a \Sigma _{ab}&= 2 \tau ^2 \jmath _b ,\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}R(g)-|Σ|g2+23=4τE,∇aΣab=2τ2ȷb,and two elliptic equations for the lapse and shift variables,2.7b\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} (\Delta - \tfrac{1}{3})N&= N \left( |\Sigma |_g^2 - \tau \eta \right) -1, \\ \Delta X^a + \text {Ric}[g]^a _b X^b&= 2 \nabla _b N \Sigma ^{ba} - \nabla ^a \widehat{N}+ 2 N \tau ^2 \jmath ^a\\&\quad - (2N \Sigma ^{bc} - \nabla ^b X^c)(\Gamma [g]^a_{bc} - \Gamma [\gamma ]^a_{bc}).\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(Δ-13)N=N|Σ|g2-τη-1,ΔXa+Ric[g]baXb=2∇bNΣba-∇aN^+2Nτ2ȷa-(2NΣbc-∇bXc)(Γ[g]bca-Γ[γ]bca).We also have evolution equations for the induced metric and trace-free part of the second fundamental form2.7c\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \partial _T g_{ab}&= 2N \Sigma _{ab} + 2\widehat{N}g_{ab} - \mathscr {L}_X g_{ab}, \\ \partial _T \Sigma _{ab}&= -2\Sigma _{ab} - N(\text {Ric}[g]_{ab} +\tfrac{2}{9}g_{ab} ) + \nabla _a \nabla _b N + 2N \Sigma _{ac} \Sigma ^c_b \\&\quad -\tfrac{1}{3} \widehat{N}g_{ab} - \widehat{N}\Sigma _{ab} - \mathscr {L}_X \Sigma _{ab} + N \tau S_{ab},\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∂Tgab=2NΣab+2N^gab-LXgab,∂TΣab=-2Σab-N(Ric[g]ab+29gab)+∇a∇bN+2NΣacΣbc-13N^gab-N^Σab-LXΣab+NτSab,and, finally, evolution equations for the fluid components:2.7d\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} u^0\partial _Tu^j&= \tau u^a \nabla _a u^j + \tau ^{-1}(u^0)^2 N\nabla ^j N+F_{u^j}, \\ u^0\partial _T u^0&=\tau u^a \nabla _a u^0 + F_{u^0} , \\ u^0 \partial _T \rho&= \tau u^a \nabla _a \rho + \rho F_\rho .\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}u0∂Tuj=τua∇auj+τ-1(u0)2N∇jN+Fuj,u0∂Tu0=τua∇au0+Fu0,u0∂Tρ=τua∇aρ+ρFρ.

Using notation from [2, 3] we introduce new variables which allow us to rewrite (2.7c).

Definition 2.8

(Perturbation variables h, v, w and geometric source terms \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F_h, F_v$$\end{document}Fh,Fv) Define the variables\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} h_{ab}:=g_{ab}-\gamma _{ab}, \quad v_{ab}:=6\Sigma _{ab}, \quad w :=N/3, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}hab:=gab-γab,vab:=6Σab,w:=N/3,and the geometric source terms\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} (F_h )_{ab}&:=2\widehat{N}g_{ab} + h_{ac}\hat{\nabla }_b X^c + h_{cb}\hat{\nabla }_a X^c , \\ (F_v )_{ab}&:=\nabla _a \nabla _b N + 2N \Sigma _{ac} \Sigma ^c_b-\tfrac{1}{3} \widehat{N}g_{ab} - \widehat{N}\Sigma _{ab}+ N \tau S_{ab}\\&\quad - v_{ac}\hat{\nabla }_b X^c - v_{cb}\hat{\nabla }_a X^c. \end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}(Fh)ab:=2N^gab+hac∇^bXc+hcb∇^aXc,(Fv)ab:=∇a∇bN+2NΣacΣbc-13N^gab-N^Σab+NτSab-vac∇^bXc-vcb∇^aXc.

We start with the following identity from [2]:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathscr {L}_X g_{ab} = X^c \hat{\nabla }_c g_{ab} +g_{ac}\hat{\nabla }_b X^c + g_{cb}\hat{\nabla }_a X^c. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}LXgab=Xc∇^cgab+gac∇^bXc+gcb∇^aXc.Due to rigidity properties of negative Einstein manifolds in three spatial dimensions (see e.g. [3, §1.1]), we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _T \gamma = 0$$\end{document}∂Tγ=0. Thus the Eq. (2.7c) reduce to2.8\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \partial _T h_{ab}&= wv_{ab} - X^m \hat{\nabla }_m h_{ab} + F_h, \\ \partial _T v_{ab}&= -2v_{ab} - 9 w\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }h_{ab} - X^c\hat{\nabla }_c v_{ab} +6F_v.\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∂Thab=wvab-Xm∇^mhab+Fh,∂Tvab=-2vab-9wLg,γhab-Xc∇^cvab+6Fv.In Sect. 8 we will also write the first equation in (2.8) as2.9\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} & \partial _T h_{ab} = wv_{ab} + 2\widehat{N}g_{ab} - (\mathscr {L}_X g)_{ab} = wv_{ab} + 2\widehat{N}\nonumber \\ & g_{ab} - g_{am}\nabla _b X^m - g_{bm} \nabla _a X^m. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∂Thab=wvab+2N^gab-(LXg)ab=wvab+2N^gab-gam∇bXm-gbm∇aXm.Hereon we use the differential Eq. (2.7b), (2.7d) and (2.8) to analyse the solutions to our Einstein-Dust system.

Preliminary Definitions

In this section we present several preliminary definitions concerning Sobolev spaces, norms, elliptic estimates and energy functionals. All of this is standard except for Definitions 3.9 and 3.11 where we introduce the fluid derivative \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _{\textbf{u}}$$\end{document}∂u and then the energy functionals for the geometric variables involving this fluid derivative.

Function Spaces and Norms

Definition 3.1

(\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2_{g,\gamma }$$\end{document}Lg,γ2-inner product) Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu _g = \sqrt{\det g}$$\end{document}μg=detg denote the volume element on (M, g), similarly for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu _\gamma $$\end{document}μγ. Let V, P be (0, 2)-tensors on M. Define an inner product by\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \langle V, P\rangle _\gamma :=V_{ij} P_{kl}\gamma ^{ik}\gamma ^{jl}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}⟨V,P⟩γ:=VijPklγikγjl,and define a mixed \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2$$\end{document}L2-scalar product\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} (V, P)_{L^2(g, \gamma )} :=\int _M \langle V, P\rangle _\gamma \, \mu _g , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(V,P)L2(g,γ):=∫M⟨V,P⟩γμg,with corresponding norm \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \Vert V\Vert _{L^2_{g,\gamma }}^2 :=(V, V)_{L^2(g, \gamma )}. $$\end{document}‖V‖Lg,γ22:=(V,V)L2(g,γ).

The following definition follows notation first introduced in [3].

Definition 3.2

(\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\,\textrm{Riem}\,}}{[}\gamma {]}\circ $$\end{document}Riem[γ]∘ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }$$\end{document}Lg,γ) Let V be a symmetric (0, 2)-tensor on M. Define the tensorial contraction\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} ({{\,\textrm{Riem}\,}}[\gamma ]\circ V)_{ij} :={{\,\textrm{Riem}\,}}[\gamma ]_{iajb}\gamma ^{aa'} \gamma ^{bb'}V_{a'b'} \,, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(Riem[γ]∘V)ij:=Riem[γ]iajbγaa′γbb′Va′b′,where, following the convention of [2], the Riemann tensor is defined by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$[\hat{\nabla }_a,\hat{\nabla }_i]V_b =(\hat{\nabla }_a\hat{\nabla }_i - \hat{\nabla }_i\hat{\nabla }_a) V_b:=-\text {Riem}[\gamma ]^c _{bai}V_c$$\end{document}[∇^a,∇^i]Vb=(∇^a∇^i-∇^i∇^a)Vb:=-Riem[γ]baicVc. Define the following differential operators\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \hat{\Delta }_{g, \gamma }V_{ij}&:=(\sqrt{\det g})^{-1}\hat{\nabla }_a\big (\sqrt{\det g}\cdot g^{ab} \hat{\nabla }_b V_{ij}\big ), \\ \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }V_{ij}&:=-\hat{\Delta }_{g, \gamma } V_{ij} - 2 ({{\,\textrm{Riem}\,}}[\gamma ]\circ V)_{ij} .\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}Δ^g,γVij:=(detg)-1∇^a(detg·gab∇^bVij),Lg,γVij:=-Δ^g,γVij-2(Riem[γ]∘V)ij.

By using the gauge condition (2.1), the operator \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\hat{\Delta }_{g, \gamma }$$\end{document}Δ^g,γ can be rewritten as\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \hat{\Delta }_{g, \gamma }V_{cd}= g^{ab}\hat{\nabla }_a \hat{\nabla }_b V_{cd}- H^a \hat{\nabla }_a V_{cd}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Δ^g,γVcd=gab∇^a∇^bVcd-Ha∇^aVcd.The operator \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }$$\end{document}Lg,γ is self-adjoint with respect to the mixed \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2$$\end{document}L2-scalar product (see e.g. [2])3.1\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} (\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }V, P)_{L^2(g, \gamma )} = (V, \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }P)_{L^2(g, \gamma )}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(Lg,γV,P)L2(g,γ)=(V,Lg,γP)L2(g,γ).A self-adjoint elliptic operator on a compact manifold has a discrete spectrum of eigenvalues. Using eigenvalue estimates from [17], we are led to the following result:

Proposition 3.3

(Estimates on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda _0$$\end{document}λ0) Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(M,\gamma )$$\end{document}(M,γ) be a negative Einstein three-manifold with Einstein constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k=-2/9$$\end{document}k=-2/9. Then the smallest eigenvalue of the operator \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }$$\end{document}Lg,γ satisfies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda _0\ge 1/9$$\end{document}λ0≥1/9 and the operator also has trivial kernel \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ker (\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma })=\{0\}$$\end{document}ker(Lg,γ)={0}.

Definition 3.4

(Sobolev norms) Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k \in \mathbb {Z}_{\ge 0}$$\end{document}k∈Z≥0. For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\kappa $$\end{document}κ a Riemannian metric on M, f a function and V a (1, 1)-tensor, define\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} |\nabla ^k f|_\kappa ^2&:=\kappa ^{a_1 b_1} \cdots \kappa ^{a_k b_k} (\nabla _{a_1}\cdots \nabla _{a_k} f )\cdot (\nabla _{b_1}\cdots \nabla _{b_k} f ), \\ |\nabla ^k V|_\kappa ^2&:=\kappa _{ij} \kappa ^{kl} \kappa ^{a_1 b_1} \cdots \kappa ^{a_k b_k} (\nabla _{a_1}\cdots \nabla _{a_k} V^i _k)\cdot (\nabla _{b_1}\cdots \nabla _{b_k} V^j _l).\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}|∇kf|κ2:=κa1b1⋯κakbk(∇a1⋯∇akf)·(∇b1⋯∇bkf),|∇kV|κ2:=κijκklκa1b1⋯κakbk(∇a1⋯∇akVki)·(∇b1⋯∇bkVlj).The obvious extension to (p, q)-tensors holds. We write\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \Vert V \Vert _{H^k}&= \Big (\sum _{0\le \ell \le k}\int _M |\nabla ^\ell V|_g^2 \, \mu _g\Big )^{1/2}.\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖V‖Hk=(∑0≤ℓ≤k∫M|∇ℓV|g2μg)1/2.Note that under a global smallness assumption on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g-\gamma $$\end{document}g-γ guaranteed by the bootstrap assumptions, we have the norm equivalence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert \cdot \Vert _{L^2} \cong \Vert \cdot \Vert _{L^2_{g,\gamma }}$$\end{document}‖·‖L2≅‖·‖Lg,γ2.

Remark 3.5

When we write \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert u \Vert _{H^{k}}$$\end{document}‖u‖Hk we are denoting a sum only over the spatial components of the velocity vector-field \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^\mu $$\end{document}uμ.

We frequently, and without comment, use the following product estimate:

Lemma 3.6

(Sobolev product estimates) If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s>n/p = 3/2$$\end{document}s>n/p=3/2 then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert uv \Vert _{H^s} \lesssim \Vert u \Vert _{H^s}\Vert v \Vert _{H^s}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖uv‖Hs≲‖u‖Hs‖v‖Hs.

We conclude this subsection with a result concerning elliptic regularity, see e.g. [4, App. H].

Lemma 3.7

(Elliptic regularity using \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }$$\end{document}Lg,γ) Let V be a symmetric (0, 2)-tensor on M. There exist constants \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C_1, C_2>0$$\end{document}C1,C2>0 such, that for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s \in \mathbb {Z}_{\ge 0}$$\end{document}s∈Z≥0,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} C_1\Vert V \Vert _{H^{k+2s}} \le \Vert \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^s V\Vert _{H^k} \le C_2\Vert V\Vert _{H^{k+2s}}\,, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}C1‖V‖Hk+2s≤‖Lg,γsV‖Hk≤C2‖V‖Hk+2s,where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^s$$\end{document}Lg,γs denotes s-copies of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }$$\end{document}Lg,γ.

Energy for the Perturbation of the Geometry

As noted in [2], in the spatially harmonic gauge (2.1) we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \text {Ric}[g]_{ab}+\frac{2}{9} g_{ab} = \frac{1}{2} \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }(g-\gamma )_{ab} + J_{ab}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Ric[g]ab+29gab=12Lg,γ(g-γ)ab+Jab,where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$J_{ab}$$\end{document}Jab are higher-order terms (writen as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S_{ab}$$\end{document}Sab in [2, pg. 22]) satisfying, for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k\ge 1$$\end{document}k≥1,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert J\Vert _{H^{k-1}}\le C \Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^k}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖J‖Hk-1≤C‖g-γ‖Hk.Following [1, 3], we define an energy for the geometric perturbation of the first and second fundamental forms by using \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }$$\end{document}Lg,γ. This energy will fulfill a strong decay estimate enabled by the inclusion of certain correction terms \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Gamma _{(m)}$$\end{document}Γ(m).

Definition 3.8

(Geometric energy \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${E^g}_k$$\end{document}Egk) Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda _0$$\end{document}λ0 be the lowest eigenvalue of the operator \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }$$\end{document}Lg,γ, with lower bounds given in Proposition 3.3. We define the correction parameter \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha =\alpha (\lambda _0,\delta _\alpha )$$\end{document}α=α(λ0,δα) by\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \alpha :={\left\{ \begin{array}{ll} 1& \lambda _0>1/9\\ 1-\delta _\alpha & \lambda _0=1/9, \end{array}\right. } \end{aligned}$$\end{document}α:=1λ0>1/91-δαλ0=1/9,where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta _\alpha =\sqrt{1-9(\lambda _0-\varepsilon ')}$$\end{document}δα=1-9(λ0-ε′) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1\gg \varepsilon '>0$$\end{document}1≫ε′>0 remains a variable to be determined in the course of the argument to follow. By fixing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon '$$\end{document}ε′ once and for all, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta _\alpha $$\end{document}δα can be made suitably small when necessary. The corresponding correction constant, relevant for defining the corrected energies, is defined by\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} c_E:={\left\{ \begin{array}{ll} 1& \lambda _0>1/9\\ 9(\lambda _0-\varepsilon ')& \lambda _0=1/9. \end{array}\right. } \end{aligned}$$\end{document}cE:=1λ0>1/99(λ0-ε′)λ0=1/9.We are now ready to define the energy for the geometric perturbation. For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$m,k \in \mathbb {Z}_{\ge 1}$$\end{document}m,k∈Z≥1 let\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {E}_{(m)}&:=\frac{1}{2}\left( v,\mathcal {L}_{g,\gamma }^{m-1}( v) \right) _{L^2_{g,\gamma }}+\frac{9}{2}\left( h,\mathcal {L}_{g,\gamma }^{m}(h)\right) _{L^2_{g,\gamma }},\qquad \Gamma _{(m)}:=\left( v,\mathcal {L}_{g,\gamma }^{m-1}(h)\right) _{L^2_{g,\gamma }}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}E(m):=12v,Lg,γm-1(v)Lg,γ2+92h,Lg,γm(h)Lg,γ2,Γ(m):=v,Lg,γm-1(h)Lg,γ2.The energy measuring the geometric perturbation is then defined by\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {E^g}_k:=\sum _{1\le m\le k} \big (\mathcal {E}_{(m)}+c_E\Gamma _{(m)}\big ). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Egk:=∑1≤m≤k(E(m)+cEΓ(m)).

We will see later that the corrected geometric energy \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${E^g}_k$$\end{document}Egk is in fact coercive over the standard Sobolev norms of the geometric variables \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g, \Sigma $$\end{document}g,Σ.

Definition 3.9

(Operators \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\hat{\partial }_0, \partial _{\textbf{u}}$$\end{document}∂^0,∂u) We define the operators on M\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \hat{\partial }_0:=\partial _T + \mathcal {L}_X, \qquad \partial _{\textbf{u}}:=u^0 \partial _T - \tau u^a \hat{\nabla }_a . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∂^0:=∂T+LX,∂u:=u0∂T-τua∇^a.

The following identity, taken from [8], holds for some function f on M:3.2\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \partial _T \int _M f \mu _g = 3 \int _M \widehat{N}f \mu _g + \int _M \hat{\partial }_0(f) \mu _g \,. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∂T∫Mfμg=3∫MN^fμg+∫M∂^0(f)μg.

Remark 3.10

(Regularity parameters \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell , N$$\end{document}ℓ,N) At top-order our bootstrap assumptions will involve Sobolev norms \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$H^N$$\end{document}HN where N is a large integer. It is convenient to require N to be odd so that we can introduce \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell \in \mathbb {Z}$$\end{document}ℓ∈Z satisfying \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell =\frac{N-1}{2}$$\end{document}ℓ=N-12.

We end this section with the top-order geometric energy for the geometric variables \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g, \Sigma $$\end{document}g,Σ, which crucially involves the fluid operator \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _{\textbf{u}}$$\end{document}∂u. This energy will also fulfill a strong decay estimate enabled by the inclusion of the correction terms.

(\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} The Bootstrap Argument

In this section we first state the local-existence theory for the Einstein-Dust system in CMCSH gauge. Then we introduce the bootstrap assumptions on our solution and give some immediate consequences of these estimates.

Local Existence

Theorem 4.1

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N\ge 6$$\end{document}N≥6. Consider CMC initial data \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(g_0,k_0,N_0,X_0,\rho _0,u_0)\in H^{N}\times H^{N-1}\times H^N\times H^N\times H^{N-2}\times H^{N-1}$$\end{document}(g0,k0,N0,X0,ρ0,u0)∈HN×HN-1×HN×HN×HN-2×HN-1 at \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T=T_0$$\end{document}T=T0 such that the constraints (2.7a) hold. Then there exists a unique classical solution \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(g,k,N,X,\rho ,u)$$\end{document}(g,k,N,X,ρ,u) on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$[T_0,T_+)$$\end{document}[T0,T+) for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T_+>T_0$$\end{document}T+>T0 to the system (2.7), which is consequently also a solution to the Einstein-Dust equations. The components have the following regularity features\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} g,N,X \in C^0([T_0,T_+),H^N)\cap C^1([T_0,T_+),H^{N-1}),\\ k\in C^0([T_0,T_+),H^{N-1})\cap C^1([T_0,T_+),H^{N-2}),\\ u\in C^{0}([T_0,T_+],H^{N-1}),\\ \rho ,\partial _ {\textbf{u}}\rho ,\partial _{\textbf{u}}u \in C^{0}([T_0,T_+],H^{N-2}).\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}g,N,X∈C0([T0,T+),HN)∩C1([T0,T+),HN-1),k∈C0([T0,T+),HN-1)∩C1([T0,T+),HN-2),u∈C0([T0,T+],HN-1),ρ,∂uρ,∂uu∈C0([T0,T+],HN-2).Furthermore, the time of existence and the norms of the solution depend continuously on the initial data. For the maximal time of existence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T_\infty $$\end{document}T∞ we have either \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T_\infty =+\infty $$\end{document}T∞=+∞ or\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \lim _{T\nearrow T_\infty } \sup _{[T_0,T_\infty ]}&\Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^N}+ \Vert \Sigma \Vert _{H^{N-1}}+\Vert N-3\Vert _{H^N}+\Vert X\Vert _{H^N}\\&+\Vert \partial _T N\Vert _{H^{N-1}}+\Vert \partial _T X\Vert _{H^{N-1}}+\Vert \rho \Vert _{H^{N-2}}+|\tau |\Vert u\Vert _{H^{N-1}}> \delta (\gamma ),\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}limT↗T∞sup[T0,T∞]‖g-γ‖HN+‖Σ‖HN-1+‖N-3‖HN+‖X‖HN+‖∂TN‖HN-1+‖∂TX‖HN-1+‖ρ‖HN-2+|τ|‖u‖HN-1>δ(γ),where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta (\gamma )$$\end{document}δ(γ) is a positive fixed constant depending only on the background metric.

Proof

The proof follows analogous to [16, Theorem 3.5] where the control on the lapse and shift are replaced by the elliptic techniques applied in [11]. The adaption of the regularity scheme to avoid the loss of derivatives from [16] to the present case is executed in detail in the global analysis discussed in the remainder of the paper. The smallness condition in the continuation criterion stems from a smallness requirement in applying the corresponding elliptic equation for the lapse. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Remark 4.2

Since we apply the local-existence theorem only for data close to the background solution the smallness condition required to extend the solution for arbitrarily large times is automatically fulfilled when our smallness conditions hold, which we prove by a bootstrap argument. Since the smallness parameter of the continuation criterion depends only on the background geometry we can choose the smallness of the initial data, which we do accordingly without mentioning it explicitly again.

Bootstrap Assumptions

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu , \lambda $$\end{document}μ,λ be fixed positive constants with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \mu \ll 1$$\end{document}μ≪1 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda <1$$\end{document}λ<1. We assume that, for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T_0 \le T\le T'$$\end{document}T0≤T≤T′, the following bootstrap assumptions hold (2.7):4.1\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \Vert {g-\gamma }\Vert _{H^N} +\Vert \Sigma \Vert _{H^{N-1}}&\le C \varepsilon e^{-\lambda T},\\ \Vert N-3\Vert _{H^{N}}+\Vert X\Vert _{H^{N}}&\le C\varepsilon e^{-T},\\ \Vert \partial _T N\Vert _{H^{N-1}}+\Vert \partial _T X\Vert _{H^{N-1}}&\le C\varepsilon e^{-T},\\ \Vert \rho \Vert _{H^{N-2}}&\le C \varepsilon , \\ \Vert u \Vert _{H^{N-1}}&\le C \varepsilon e^{\mu T}.\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖g-γ‖HN+‖Σ‖HN-1≤Cεe-λT,‖N-3‖HN+‖X‖HN≤Cεe-T,‖∂TN‖HN-1+‖∂TX‖HN-1≤Cεe-T,‖ρ‖HN-2≤Cε,‖u‖HN-1≤CεeμT.there \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T'<T_\infty $$\end{document}T′<T∞ is fixed. We hereon assume that (4.1) hold and do not repeat this fact. Recall also Remark 3.5 regarding the norm on u.

Definition 4.3

(\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda (T)$$\end{document}Λ(T)) It is convenient to introduce the notation\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Lambda (T) :=\Vert \widehat{N}\Vert _{H^N} + \Vert X\Vert _{H^N} + \Vert \partial _T N \Vert _{H^{N-1}} + \Vert \partial _T X \Vert _{H^{N-1}} + |\tau | \Vert \rho \Vert _{H^{N-2}} + \tau ^2 \Vert u \Vert _{H^{N-1}}^2. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Λ(T):=‖N^‖HN+‖X‖HN+‖∂TN‖HN-1+‖∂TX‖HN-1+|τ|‖ρ‖HN-2+τ2‖u‖HN-12.Note that, under the bootstrap assumptions (4.1), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda (T) \lesssim \varepsilon e^{-T}$$\end{document}Λ(T)≲εe-T.

We state some immediate consequences of the bootstrap assumptions regarding \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^0$$\end{document}u0 which we use without further comment. Using Lemma 2.3, we have4.2\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \Vert \hat{u}^0\Vert _{H^{N-1}}&\lesssim \varepsilon , \\ \Vert \nabla \hat{u}^0\Vert _{H^{N-2}}&\lesssim \Vert \nabla N \Vert _{H^{N-2}} + \tau ^2 \Vert u^a \nabla u^a \Vert _{H^{N-2}} + \text {h.o.t} \lesssim \Lambda (T).\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖u^0‖HN-1≲ε,‖∇u^0‖HN-2≲‖∇N‖HN-2+τ2‖ua∇ua‖HN-2+h.o.t≲Λ(T).Note the first estimate does not pick up any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu -$$\end{document}μ-loss. By the Sobolev embedding \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$H^2 \hookrightarrow L^\infty $$\end{document}H2↪L∞, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert \hat{u}^0\Vert _{L^\infty }= \Vert u^0-1/3\Vert _{L^\infty }\le 1/10$$\end{document}‖u^0‖L∞=‖u0-1/3‖L∞≤1/10 and thus\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert u^0\Vert _{L^\infty }\lesssim 1, \quad \Vert u^0\Vert _{L^\infty }^{-1} \lesssim 1. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖u0‖L∞≲1,‖u0‖L∞-1≲1.The next Lemma concerning the dust matter components is indicative of the good behaviour that, as we discussed in Sect. 1.2.4, we roughly expect as the speed of sound is reduced.

Lemma 4.4

(Estimates on matter components) We have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} |\tau |\big (\Vert E\Vert _{H^{N-2}} + \Vert \eta \Vert _{H^{N-2}}+ \Vert S\Vert _{H^{N-2}} \big )&\lesssim \Lambda (T),\\ |\tau |^2 \Vert \jmath \Vert _{H^{N-2}}\lesssim \varepsilon e^{(-1+\mu )T} \Lambda (T), \quad |\tau |^3 \Vert \underline{T}\Vert _{H^{N-2}}&\lesssim \Lambda (T)^2.\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}|τ|(‖E‖HN-2+‖η‖HN-2+‖S‖HN-2)≲Λ(T),|τ|2‖ȷ‖HN-2≲εe(-1+μ)TΛ(T),|τ|3‖T_‖HN-2≲Λ(T)2.

Proof

The estimates are immediate by distributing derivatives across the terms written in Definition 2.6 and using Lemma 3.6. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Lemma 4.5

(Geometric coercivity estimate) Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s\in \mathbb {Z}$$\end{document}s∈Z such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1\le s \le N-1$$\end{document}1≤s≤N-1. There is a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta >0$$\end{document}δ>0 and a constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C>0$$\end{document}C>0 such that for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(g,\Sigma )\in B_{\delta }((\gamma ,0))$$\end{document}(g,Σ)∈Bδ((γ,0)) the following inequality holds\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^{s}}^2 + \Vert \Sigma \Vert _{H^{s-1}}^2 \le C E^g_{s}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖g-γ‖Hs2+‖Σ‖Hs-12≤CEsg.

Proof

The proof of this lemma follows verbatim from [1, Lemma 19], which itself follows from [3, Lemma 7.2], and the eigenvalue estimates referred to in Proposition 3.3. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

The next Lemma is actually only used once in our entire argument. Its significance lies in the fact that it allows us to convert the quadratic derivative term \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\nabla V)^2$$\end{document}(∇V)2 appearing in the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$H^1$$\end{document}H1 Sobolev norm into just one second-order derivative as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$V \cdot \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }V$$\end{document}V·Lg,γV, which will more naturally be controlled by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E^g_{\partial _{\textbf{u}}, N-1} $$\end{document}E∂u,N-1g.

Lemma 4.6

Let V be a symmetric (0, 2)-tensor on M. Then,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert V\Vert _{H^1} \lesssim \Vert V\Vert _{L^2} + (V, \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }V)_{L^2_{g,\gamma }}^{1/2}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖V‖H1≲‖V‖L2+(V,Lg,γV)Lg,γ21/2.

Proof

We integrate by parts, use the closeness between the g and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\gamma $$\end{document}γ metrics and the boundedness of the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {Riem}[\gamma ]$$\end{document}Riem[γ] components:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert V\Vert _{H^1}^2&= \Vert V\Vert _{L^2}^2 + \int _M g^{ab} g^{ij} g^{kl} \nabla _a V_{ik} \nabla _b V_{jl} \, \mu _g \\ &\le \Vert V\Vert _{L^2}^2 +\Big | \int _M g^{ab} g^{ij} g^{kl} V_{ik} \nabla _a \nabla _b V_{jl} \, \mu _g \Big | \\ &\le \Vert V\Vert _{L^2}^2 + \Big | \int _M \langle V,\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }V \rangle _\gamma \, \mu _g + 2 \int _M \langle V, \text{ Riem }[\gamma ]\circ V \rangle _\gamma \, \mu _g \Big | \\ &\lesssim \Vert V\Vert _{L^2}^2 + (V, \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }V)_{L^2_{g,\gamma }}.\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖V‖H12=‖V‖L22+∫Mgabgijgkl∇aVik∇bVjlμg≤‖V‖L22+|∫MgabgijgklVik∇a∇bVjlμg|≤‖V‖L22+|∫M⟨V,Lg,γV⟩γμg+2∫M⟨V,Riem[γ]∘V⟩γμg|≲‖V‖L22+(V,Lg,γV)Lg,γ2.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Preliminary Estimates

This section is concerned with deriving several preliminary estimates that are required for our later energy inequalities. There are estimates on commutator terms (Sects. 5.1, 5.4), estimates on matter terms (Sect. 5.3) and also estimates on geometric variables (Sects. 5.2, 5.5). We also present an integration by parts Lemma 5.3, in particular (5.6c), which later plays an important role in removing various critical terms that arise during the energy estimates.

First Commutator Estimates

In this subsection we let V be an arbitrary symmetric (0, 2)-tensor and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi $$\end{document}ϕ a scalar, unless otherwise specified. We begin with the identity5.1\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} [\partial _T, \nabla _a]V_{i j} = (-\partial _T \Gamma ^c_{ai})V_{c j} + (-\partial _T \Gamma ^c_{aj})V_{i c}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}[∂T,∇a]Vij=(-∂TΓaic)Vcj+(-∂TΓajc)Vic.The terms \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _T \Gamma [g]$$\end{document}∂TΓ[g] can be estimated (see [1, (10.12)]), for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le k \le N-2$$\end{document}0≤k≤N-2, by:5.2\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert \partial _T \Gamma (g)\Vert _{H^k} \lesssim \Vert \Sigma \Vert _{H^{k+1}} + \Vert X\Vert _{H^{k+2}} + \Vert \widehat{N}\Vert _{H^{k+1}}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∂TΓ(g)‖Hk≲‖Σ‖Hk+1+‖X‖Hk+2+‖N^‖Hk+1.We also have the following commutator identities:5.3\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} [\partial _T,\nabla _{a_1}\cdots \nabla _{a_k}]\phi&= [\partial _T,\nabla _{a_1}]\nabla _{a_2}\cdots \nabla _{a_k} \phi + \nabla _{a_1} [\partial _T,\nabla _{a_2}]\nabla _{a_3}\cdots \nabla _{a_k} \phi \nonumber \\&\quad +\ldots + \nabla _{a_1}\cdots \nabla _{a_{k-2}} [\partial _T,\nabla _{a_{k-1}}] \nabla \end{aligned}$$\end{document}[∂T,∇a1⋯∇ak]ϕ=[∂T,∇a1]∇a2⋯∇akϕ+∇a1[∂T,∇a2]∇a3⋯∇akϕ+…+∇a1⋯∇ak-2[∂T,∇ak-1]∇akϕ+∇a1⋯∇ak-1[∂T,∇ak]ϕ,5.4\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym}
