+\ldots + \nabla _{a_1}\cdots \nabla _{a_{k-2}} [\nabla _i,\nabla _{a_{k-1}}] \nabla _{a_k} \phi + \nabla _{a_1}\cdots \nabla _{a_{k-1}} [\nabla _i,\nabla _{a_{k}}] \phi . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}[∇i,∇a1⋯∇ak]ϕ=[∇i,∇a1]∇a2⋯∇akϕ+∇a1[∇i,∇a2]∇a3⋯∇akϕ+…+∇a1⋯∇ak-2[∇i,∇ak-1]∇akϕ+∇a1⋯∇ak-1[∇i,∇ak]ϕ.Note the last terms in each of (5.3) and (5.4) will in fact vanish since the metric g is torsion free.

In the next part of this subsection we state an important estimate, given in (5.5), that allows us to turn background \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\hat{\nabla }$$\end{document}∇^ derivatives into dynamical \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nabla $$\end{document}∇ ones.

Definition 5.1

(Difference tensor \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Upsilon $$\end{document}Υ) Recalling that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nabla $$\end{document}∇ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\hat{\nabla }$$\end{document}∇^ are the Levi-Civita symbols of g and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\gamma $$\end{document}γ respectively, we define \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Upsilon $$\end{document}Υ a (1,2)-tensor by\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Upsilon ^a_{bc} :=\Gamma ^a_{bc}[g]-\Gamma ^a_{bc}[\gamma ]. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Υbca:=Γbca[g]-Γbca[γ].

Let V be a vector and P a one-form. Then we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \nabla _a V^{i}= \hat{\nabla }_a V^i + \Upsilon ^i_{ja}V^{j}, \qquad \nabla _a P_i= \hat{\nabla }_a P_i - \Upsilon ^j_{ia}P_j. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∇aVi=∇^aVi+ΥjaiVj,∇aPi=∇^aPi-ΥiajPj.We will often schematically write tensorial contractions using \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$*$$\end{document}∗. For example,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \nabla _a V^{i}= \hat{\nabla }_a V^i + \Upsilon ^i_{ja}V^{j}, \quad \text { becomes } \quad \nabla V = \hat{\nabla }V + \Upsilon *V. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∇aVi=∇^aVi+ΥjaiVj,becomes∇V=∇^V+Υ∗V.In local coordinates the components of the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Upsilon $$\end{document}Υ tensor are given by\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Upsilon ^a_{bc}&=-\frac{1}{2} \gamma ^{ai}\left( \nabla _b \gamma _{ci} + \nabla _c \gamma _{ai} - \nabla _i \gamma _{bc} \right) =\frac{1}{2} \gamma ^{ai}\left( \nabla _b h_{ci} + \nabla _c h_{ai} - \nabla _i h_{bc} \right) .\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}Υbca=-12γai∇bγci+∇cγai-∇iγbc=12γai∇bhci+∇chai-∇ihbc.

Lemma 5.2

For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0\le k \le N-2$$\end{document}0≤k≤N-2, we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert [\hat{\nabla }, \nabla ] V\Vert _{H^k}&\lesssim \Vert V\Vert _{H^k} + \varepsilon e^{-\lambda T}\Vert V\Vert _{H^{k+1}}.\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖[∇^,∇]V‖Hk≲‖V‖Hk+εe-λT‖V‖Hk+1.

Proof

First we see that from Definition 5.1, for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le k \le N-1$$\end{document}0≤k≤N-1,5.5\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert \Upsilon \Vert _{H^k} \lesssim \Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^{k+1}}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖Υ‖Hk≲‖g-γ‖Hk+1.Thus we compute,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} [\hat{\nabla }_c, \nabla _a] V_{i j}&= [\hat{\nabla }_c, \hat{\nabla }_a] V_{i j}+ \Upsilon ^b_{ca}\hat{\nabla }_b V_{i j} - \big ((\hat{\nabla }_c \Upsilon ^b_{ai})V_{b j} + (\hat{\nabla }_c \Upsilon ^b_{aj}) V_{i b}\big ).\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}[∇^c,∇a]Vij=[∇^c,∇^a]Vij+Υcab∇^bVij-((∇^cΥaib)Vbj+(∇^cΥajb)Vib).Using the boundedness of the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {Riem}[\gamma ]$$\end{document}Riem[γ] components, the required estimate then follows. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

We end this subsection with some very useful estimates that come from integration by parts.

Lemma 5.3

(Integration by parts) Let T be an arbitrary vectorfield and V, P arbitrary symmetric (0, 2)-tensors, on M. Then 5.6a\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} (\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }V, P)_{L^2(g, \gamma )} = \int _M \langle g^{ab} \hat{\nabla }_a V, \hat{\nabla }_b P \rangle _\gamma \mu _g -2 \int _M \langle \text {Riem}[\gamma ]_{\bullet a\bullet b} V^{ab}, P\rangle _\gamma \mu _g . \nonumber \\ \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(Lg,γV,P)L2(g,γ)=∫M⟨gab∇^aV,∇^bP⟩γμg-2∫M⟨Riem[γ]∙a∙bVab,P⟩γμg.and5.6b\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \big | (T^a \hat{\nabla }_a V, P)_{L^2_{g,\gamma }} \big |&\lesssim \Vert T \Vert _{H^3} \Vert V \Vert _{L^2} \Vert P\Vert _{H^1}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}|(Ta∇^aV,P)Lg,γ2|≲‖T‖H3‖V‖L2‖P‖H1,5.6c\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \big | (T^a \hat{\nabla }_a V, V)_{L^2_{g,\gamma }} \big |&\lesssim \Vert T \Vert _{H^3} \Vert V \Vert _{L^2}^2. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}|(Ta∇^aV,V)Lg,γ2|≲‖T‖H3‖V‖L22.

Proof

The proof of (5.6a) follows by using the gauge condition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$H^a = 0$$\end{document}Ha=0. To show (5.6b), recall that the Jacobi identity implies that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \hat{\nabla }_a \sqrt{\det g} = \frac{1}{2} \sqrt{\det g} \cdot g^{ij}(\hat{\nabla }_a g_{ij}). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∇^adetg=12detg·gij(∇^agij).Using this we find that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} (T^a \hat{\nabla }_a V, P)_{L^2_{g,\gamma }}&= \int _M \gamma ^{ij}\gamma ^{kl} T^a (\hat{\nabla }_a V_{ik}) P_{jl} \, \sqrt{\det g} \\&= -\int _M \gamma ^{ij}\gamma ^{kl} \hat{\nabla }_a \left( T^a P_{jl} \frac{\sqrt{\det g}}{\sqrt{\det \gamma }}\right) V_{ik} \sqrt{\det \gamma } \\ &= - (V, T^a \hat{\nabla }_a P)_{L^2_{g,\gamma }} - ((\hat{\nabla }_a T^a) V, P)_{L^2_{g,\gamma }}\\&\quad - \tfrac{1}{2} (V, (T^a g^{bc}\hat{\nabla }_a g_{bc}) P)_{L^2_{g,\gamma }}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(Ta∇^aV,P)Lg,γ2=∫MγijγklTa(∇^aVik)Pjldetg=-∫Mγijγkl∇^aTaPjldetgdetγVikdetγ=-(V,Ta∇^aP)Lg,γ2-((∇^aTa)V,P)Lg,γ2-12(V,(Tagbc∇^agbc)P)Lg,γ2.Thus,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \big | (T^a \hat{\nabla }_a V, P)_{L^2_{g,\gamma }} \big |&= \big | - (V, T^a \hat{\nabla }_a P)_{L^2_{g,\gamma }} - ((\hat{\nabla }_a T^a) V, P)_{L^2_{g,\gamma }} \\&\quad - \tfrac{1}{2} (V, (T^a g^{bc}\hat{\nabla }_a g_{bc}) P)_{L^2_{g,\gamma }} \big | \\ &\lesssim \big ( \Vert T^a \Vert _{H^3} + \Vert T^a \Vert _{H^2} \Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^3} \big ) \Vert V \Vert _{L^2} \Vert P\Vert _{H^1}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}|(Ta∇^aV,P)Lg,γ2|=|-(V,Ta∇^aP)Lg,γ2-((∇^aTa)V,P)Lg,γ2-12(V,(Tagbc∇^agbc)P)Lg,γ2|≲(‖Ta‖H3+‖Ta‖H2‖g-γ‖H3)‖V‖L2‖P‖H1.Crucially, in the symmetric case, we can bring one term over to the left hand side to show that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \big | (T^a \hat{\nabla }_a V, V)_{L^2_{g,\gamma }} \big |&= \big | - \tfrac{1}{2} ((\hat{\nabla }_a T^a) V, V)_{L^2_{g,\gamma }} - \tfrac{1}{4} (V, (T^a g^{bc}\hat{\nabla }_a g_{bc}) V)_{L^2_{g,\gamma }} \big | \\ &\lesssim \big ( \Vert T^a \Vert _{H^3} + \Vert T^a \Vert _{H^2} \Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^3} \big ) \Vert V \Vert _{L^2}^2.\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}|(Ta∇^aV,V)Lg,γ2|=|-12((∇^aTa)V,V)Lg,γ2-14(V,(Tagbc∇^agbc)V)Lg,γ2|≲(‖Ta‖H3+‖Ta‖H2‖g-γ‖H3)‖V‖L22.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

First Estimates on Geometric Components

We now establish some of our first estimates on the ADM variables N, X and the geometric variables \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g, \Sigma $$\end{document}g,Σ.

Lemma 5.4

(Dust derivatives of lapse and shift) For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$2\le k \le N-1 $$\end{document}2≤k≤N-1,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \Vert \partial _{\textbf{u}}N \Vert _{H^k} + \Vert \partial _{\textbf{u}}X \Vert _{H^k} \lesssim \Lambda (T).\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∂uN‖Hk+‖∂uX‖Hk≲Λ(T).

Proof

Since the lapse is a scalar, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _{\textbf{u}}N = u^0 \partial _T N - \tau u^c \nabla _c N$$\end{document}∂uN=u0∂TN-τuc∇cN. For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _{\textbf{u}}X^a$$\end{document}∂uXa we use (5.5). We find that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert \partial _{\textbf{u}}N \Vert _{H^k} + \Vert \partial _{\textbf{u}}X \Vert _{H^k} \lesssim \Vert \partial _T N \Vert _{H^k} + \Vert \partial _T X \Vert _{H^k} + \varepsilon e^{(-1+\mu ) T} \Lambda (T).\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∂uN‖Hk+‖∂uX‖Hk≲‖∂TN‖Hk+‖∂TX‖Hk+εe(-1+μ)TΛ(T).\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Lemma 5.5

(Estimates on geometric source terms) We have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert F_h \Vert _{H^k}&\lesssim \Vert \widehat{N}\Vert _{H^k} + \Vert X\Vert _{H^{k+1}}^2 + \Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^{k+1}}^2,\\&\quad 2 \le k \le N-1, \\ \Vert F_v\Vert _{H^k}&\lesssim \Vert \widehat{N}\Vert _{H^{k+2}} + |\tau | \Vert \rho \Vert _{H^k} + \Vert X\Vert _{H^{k+1}}^2 \\ &\quad + \Vert g -\gamma \Vert _{H^{k+1}}^2 + \Vert \Sigma \Vert _{H^{k}}^2,\\&\quad 2 \le k \le N-2,\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖Fh‖Hk≲‖N^‖Hk+‖X‖Hk+12+‖g-γ‖Hk+12,2≤k≤N-1,‖Fv‖Hk≲‖N^‖Hk+2+|τ|‖ρ‖Hk+‖X‖Hk+12+‖g-γ‖Hk+12+‖Σ‖Hk2,2≤k≤N-2,and thus\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert F_h \Vert _{H^{N-1}} + \Vert F_v \Vert _{H^{N-2}} \lesssim \Lambda (T) + \Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^N}^2 + \Vert \Sigma \Vert _{H^{N-1}}^2. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖Fh‖HN-1+‖Fv‖HN-2≲Λ(T)+‖g-γ‖HN2+‖Σ‖HN-12.

Proof

Using the product estimate of Lemma 3.6 and (5.5) we obtain, for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$2 \le k \le N-1$$\end{document}2≤k≤N-1,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} We can now combine the geometric source term estimates from the previous lemma with the equations of motion given in (2.8).

Lemma 5.6

(Time derivatives of g and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Sigma $$\end{document}Σ) The following estimates hold:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert \partial _T g\Vert _{H^k}&\lesssim \Vert \Sigma \Vert _{H^{k}} + \Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^{k+1}} +\Vert \widehat{N}\Vert _{H^k} + \Vert X\Vert _{H^{k+1}} ,&2\le k \le N-1, \\ \Vert \partial _T \Sigma \Vert _{H^k}&\lesssim \Vert \Sigma \Vert _{H^{k+1}} + \Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^{k+2}} +\Vert \widehat{N}\Vert _{H^{k+2}}\\&\quad + \Vert X\Vert _{H^{k+1}} + |\tau | \Vert \rho \Vert _{H^k},&2\le k \le N-2.\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∂Tg‖Hk≲‖Σ‖Hk+‖g-γ‖Hk+1+‖N^‖Hk+‖X‖Hk+1,2≤k≤N-1,‖∂TΣ‖Hk≲‖Σ‖Hk+1+‖g-γ‖Hk+2+‖N^‖Hk+2+‖X‖Hk+1+|τ|‖ρ‖Hk,2≤k≤N-2.

Proof

Using Lemma 5.5, (2.8) and (5.5) we find\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert \partial _T h \Vert _{H^k}&\lesssim \Vert \Sigma \Vert _{H^{k}} + \Vert X(\nabla h + \Upsilon *h)\Vert _{H^{k}} + \Vert F_h \Vert _{H^k} \\&\lesssim \Vert \Sigma \Vert _{H^{k}} +\Vert \widehat{N}\Vert _{H^k} + \Vert X\Vert _{H^{k+1}}^2 + \Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^{k+1}}^2\,,\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∂Th‖Hk≲‖Σ‖Hk+‖X(∇h+Υ∗h)‖Hk+‖Fh‖Hk≲‖Σ‖Hk+‖N^‖Hk+‖X‖Hk+12+‖g-γ‖Hk+12,and, using additionally Lemma 3.7,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \Vert \partial _T v\Vert _{H^k}&\lesssim \Vert \Sigma \Vert _{H^{k}} +\Vert \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }h\Vert _{H^{k}}+ \Vert X(\nabla v + \Upsilon *v)\Vert _{H^{k}} + \Vert F_v \Vert _{H^k} \\ &\lesssim \Vert \Sigma \Vert _{H^{k}} + \Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^{k+2}} +\Vert \widehat{N}\Vert _{H^{k+2}}\\&\quad + \Vert X\Vert _{H^{k+1}}^2 + \Vert \Sigma \Vert _{H^{k+1}}^2 + |\tau | \Vert \rho \Vert _{H^k}.\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∂Tv‖Hk≲‖Σ‖Hk+‖Lg,γh‖Hk+‖X(∇v+Υ∗v)‖Hk+‖Fv‖Hk≲‖Σ‖Hk+‖g-γ‖Hk+2+‖N^‖Hk+2+‖X‖Hk+12+‖Σ‖Hk+12+|τ|‖ρ‖Hk.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Corollary 5.7

(Dust derivatives of g and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Sigma $$\end{document}Σ) The following estimates hold:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \Vert \partial _{\textbf{u}}g\Vert _{H^k}&\lesssim \Vert \Sigma \Vert _{H^{k}} + \Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^{k+1}} +\Lambda (T) ,&2\le k \le N-1, \\ \Vert \partial _{\textbf{u}}\Sigma \Vert _{H^k}&\lesssim \Vert \Sigma \Vert _{H^{k+1}} + \Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^{k+2}} +\Lambda (T),&2\le k \le N-2.\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∂ug‖Hk≲‖Σ‖Hk+‖g-γ‖Hk+1+Λ(T),2≤k≤N-1,‖∂uΣ‖Hk≲‖Σ‖Hk+1+‖g-γ‖Hk+2+Λ(T),2≤k≤N-2.

Proof

Writing\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \partial _{\textbf{u}}V_{ab} = u^0 \partial _T V_{ab}- \tau u^c \nabla _c V_{ab}- \tau u^c\Upsilon ^d_{ca} V_{db} - \tau u^c\Upsilon ^d_{cb} V_{ad} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∂uVab=u0∂TVab-τuc∇cVab-τucΥcadVdb-τucΥcbdVadand using Lemma 5.6 gives the estimates. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Remark 5.8

It is unsurprising that for the geometric variables \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g, \Sigma $$\end{document}g,Σ, the fluid derivative estimates in Corollary 5.7 do not gain us any improved information compared to the time derivative estimates in Lemma 5.6. This will of course change when we consider instead estimates on certain fluid matter variables which are naturally more compatible with the fluid derivative operator \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _{\textbf{u}}$$\end{document}∂u.

First Estimates on Fluid Components

In this subsection we establish further estimates on the various matter variables. Note that we need to estimate both matter components coming from contractions with the stress energy tensor (see Definition 2.6) and the fluid source terms terms appearing in the equations of motion (see Definition 2.7).

Remark 5.11

The significance of using dust derivatives is made clear by look at the higher regularity appearing in Lemma 5.10 compared to the following Lemma 5.12 which only concerns time derivatives. In Lemma 5.10, we directly computed the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _{\textbf{u}}$$\end{document}∂u derivatives using the equations of motion, instead of doing a rough estimate by expanding \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _{\textbf{u}}\sim \partial _T + \tau u^c *\nabla $$\end{document}∂u∼∂T+τuc∗∇.

Second Commutator Estimates

We are now in a position to compute various commutator estimates which are required in the later energy estimates. We let V be a symmetric (0, 2)-tensor on M unless otherwise specified. The first lemma looks at the commutator between the dust derivative \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _{\textbf{u}}$$\end{document}∂u with other first-order differential operators.

Lemma 5.13

For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0\le k \le N-2$$\end{document}0≤k≤N-2, we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert [\partial _{\textbf{u}}, \partial _T] V\Vert _{H^k}&\lesssim \varepsilon e^{(-1+\mu ) T} \Vert V\Vert _{H^{k+1}} + \varepsilon e^{(-1+\mu )T}\Vert \partial _T V\Vert _{H^k}, \\ \Vert [\partial _{\textbf{u}}, \hat{\nabla }] V \Vert _{H^k}&\lesssim \varepsilon e^{(-1+\mu ) T} \Vert V\Vert _{H^{k+1}} +\Lambda (T)\Vert \partial _T V \Vert _{H^k} , \\ \Vert [\partial _{\textbf{u}}, \nabla ] V \Vert _{H^{k}}&\lesssim \varepsilon e^{\max \{-1+\mu , -\lambda \} T} \Vert V\Vert _{H^{k+1}} +\Lambda (T)\Vert \partial _T V\Vert _{H^{k}}.\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖[∂u,∂T]V‖Hk≲εe(-1+μ)T‖V‖Hk+1+εe(-1+μ)T‖∂TV‖Hk,‖[∂u,∇^]V‖Hk≲εe(-1+μ)T‖V‖Hk+1+Λ(T)‖∂TV‖Hk,‖[∂u,∇]V‖Hk≲εemax{-1+μ,-λ}T‖V‖Hk+1+Λ(T)‖∂TV‖Hk.

Proof

A computation gives5.10\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} The other two estimates follow in a similar way. Lemma 5.14

Proof

\end{aligned}$$\end{document}[∂u,Lg,γ]Vij=-(∂ugab)∇^a∇^bVij+Δ^g,γu^0·∂TVij+2gab∇^au^0∇^b∂TVij+τucgab(Riem[γ]back∇^kVij+4Riem[γ](i|ack∇^bVk|j)+2∇^aRiem[γ](i|bck·Vk|j))-2τgab∇^auc∇^b∇^cVij-τΔ^g,γuc·∇^cVij+2τuc∇^cRiem[γ]iajb·Vab.It is useful to write this schematically, using that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} Next we compute the identity5.13\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} [\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }, \hat{\nabla }_m]V_{ij}&= \hat{\nabla }_m g^{ab} \cdot \hat{\nabla }_a\hat{\nabla }_b V_{ij} - g^{ab} [\hat{\nabla }_a\hat{\nabla }_b, \hat{\nabla }_m]V_{ij},\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}[Lg,γ,∇^m]Vij=∇^mgab·∇^a∇^bVij-gab[∇^a∇^b,∇^m]Vij,where we are thinking of the m index as not being free (i.e. contracted with a factor of the shift \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$X^m$$\end{document}Xm). [\partial _T, \hat{\Delta }_{g, \gamma }]V_{ij} = -(\partial _T g^{ab}) \hat{\nabla }_a \hat{\nabla }_b V_{ij}.\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}[∂T,Lg,γ]Vij=-[∂T,Δ^g,γ]Vij=-(∂Tgab)∇^a∇^bVij.Using Lemma 5.6 to estimate \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _T g$$\end{document}∂Tg this gives, for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$2\le k \le N-2$$\end{document}2≤k≤N-2,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert [\partial _T, \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }] V\Vert _{H^k}&\lesssim \Vert \partial _T h\Vert _{H^k} \big (\Vert \nabla (\nabla V +\Upsilon *V)\Vert _{H^{k}} + \Vert \Upsilon (\nabla V+\Upsilon *V) \Vert _{H^{k}} \big )\\&\lesssim \varepsilon e^{-\lambda T} \Vert V \Vert _{H^{k+2}}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖[∂T,Lg,γ]V‖Hk≲‖∂Th‖Hk(‖∇(∇V+Υ∗V)‖Hk+‖Υ(∇V+Υ∗V)‖Hk)≲εe-λT‖V‖Hk+2.A similar argument holds for the cases \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k=0,1$$\end{document}k=0,1. At higher order, we obtain the identity5.15\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} The next corollary extends the commutator estimates of the previous lemma to higher-orders of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^s$$\end{document}Lg,γs, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s \in \mathbb {Z}_{\ge 1}$$\end{document}s∈Z≥1. Note that the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2$$\end{document}L2 estimate that appears in the statement will be typically applied with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$V=h$$\end{document}V=h, while the lower-order \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$H^1$$\end{document}H1 estimate will be primarily used later on with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$V = \Sigma $$\end{document}V=Σ.

Corollary 5.15

We have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert [ \partial _{\textbf{u}}, \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^s] V \Vert _{L^2}&\lesssim \varepsilon e^{\max \{-1+\mu ,-\lambda \} T}\Vert V\Vert _{H^{2s}}\\&\quad + \Lambda (T)\Vert \partial _T V\Vert _{H^{2s-1}} ,&\quad 1\le s \le \ell , \\ \Vert [ \partial _{\textbf{u}}, \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^{s-1}] V \Vert _{H^1}&\lesssim \varepsilon e^{\max \{-1+\mu ,-\lambda \} T}\Vert V\Vert _{H^{2s-1}}\\&\quad + \Lambda (T)\Vert \partial _T V\Vert _{H^{2s-2}} ,&\quad 2\le s \le \ell .\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖[∂u,Lg,γs]V‖L2≲εemax{-1+μ,-λ}T‖V‖H2s+Λ(T)‖∂TV‖H2s-1,1≤s≤ℓ,‖[∂u,Lg,γs-1]V‖H1≲εemax{-1+μ,-λ}T‖V‖H2s-1+Λ(T)‖∂TV‖H2s-2,2≤s≤ℓ.

Proof

By Lemma 5.14,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert [ \partial _{\textbf{u}}, \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^s] V \Vert _{L^2}&\lesssim \Vert [ \partial _{\textbf{u}}, \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }] \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^{s-1}V \Vert _{L^2} + \Vert [ \partial _{\textbf{u}}, \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }] \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^{s-2}V \Vert _{H^2}\\&\quad + \cdots + \Vert [ \partial _{\textbf{u}}, \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }] V \Vert _{H^{2(s-1)}} \\&\lesssim \varepsilon e^{\max \{-1+\mu ,-\lambda \} T}\Vert V\Vert _{H^{2s}} +\Lambda (T)\Vert \partial _T V\Vert _{H^{2s-1}}\\&\quad + \Lambda (T) \sum _{p=0}^{s-2}\Vert [\partial _T, \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^{s-1-p}]V\Vert _{H^{1+2p}}.\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖[∂u,Lg,γs]V‖L2≲‖[∂u,Lg,γ]Lg,γs-1V‖L2+‖[∂u,Lg,γ]Lg,γs-2V‖H2+⋯+‖[∂u,Lg,γ]V‖H2(s-1)≲εemax{-1+μ,-λ}T‖V‖H2s+Λ(T)‖∂TV‖H2s-1+Λ(T)∑p=0s-2‖[∂T,Lg,γs-1-p]V‖H1+2p.The conclusion then easily follows and the second estimate follows in the same way. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Corollary 5.16

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert \partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^{\ell -1}(\Sigma )\Vert _{L^2}&\lesssim (E^g_{N-1})^{1/2} +\Lambda (T),\\ \Vert \partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^{\ell -1}(\Sigma )\Vert _{H^1}&\lesssim \Vert \Sigma \Vert _{H^{N-1}}+ \Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^{N}} + \Lambda (T).\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∂uLg,γℓ-1(Σ)‖L2≲(EN-1g)1/2+Λ(T),‖∂uLg,γℓ-1(Σ)‖H1≲‖Σ‖HN-1+‖g-γ‖HN+Λ(T).

Proof

By the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _{\textbf{u}}\Sigma , \partial _T \Sigma $$\end{document}∂uΣ,∂TΣ estimates of Lemma 5.6, Corollary 5.7, and the previous commutator estimate of corollary 5.15\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert \partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^{\ell -1}(\Sigma )\Vert _{L^2}&\lesssim \Vert \partial _{\textbf{u}}\Sigma \Vert _{H^{N-3}} +\Vert [\partial _{\textbf{u}},\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^{\ell -1}](\Sigma )\Vert _{L^2}\\&\lesssim \Vert \Sigma \Vert _{H^{N-2}}+ \Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^{N-1}} +\Lambda (T). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∂uLg,γℓ-1(Σ)‖L2≲‖∂uΣ‖HN-3+‖[∂u,Lg,γℓ-1](Σ)‖L2≲‖Σ‖HN-2+‖g-γ‖HN-1+Λ(T).The conclusion then follows by the coercive estimate of Lemma 4.5. Similarly,5.17\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \Vert \partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^{\ell -1} \Sigma \Vert _{H^1}&\lesssim \Vert \Sigma \Vert _{H^{N-1}}+ \Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^{N}} + \Lambda (T) + \varepsilon e^{\max \{-1+\mu ,-\lambda \} T}\Vert \Sigma \Vert _{H^{N-2}} \\ &\quad + \Lambda (T)\Vert \partial _T \Sigma \Vert _{H^{N-3}} \\&\lesssim \Vert \Sigma \Vert _{H^{N-1}}+ \Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^{N}} + \Lambda (T).\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∂uLg,γℓ-1Σ‖H1≲‖Σ‖HN-1+‖g-γ‖HN+Λ(T)+εemax{-1+μ,-λ}T‖Σ‖HN-2+Λ(T)‖∂TΣ‖HN-3≲‖Σ‖HN-1+‖g-γ‖HN+Λ(T).\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Remark 5.17

Frequently in our energy estimates we will need to study the term \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert \partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^{\ell -1}(\Sigma )\Vert _{H^1}$$\end{document}‖∂uLg,γℓ-1(Σ)‖H1 appearing in the previous Corollary. However, by looking at the estimate derived in Corollary 5.16, we see that we cannot apply Lemma 4.5 to the top-order Sobolev norms \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert \Sigma \Vert _{H^{N-1}}$$\end{document}‖Σ‖HN-1 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^{N}} $$\end{document}‖g-γ‖HN. To estimate these terms by the geometric energy \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E^g_{tot}$$\end{document}Etotg we will instead need to use the auxiliary elliptic estimates established in Sect. 6.

We conclude this subsection with a commutator estimate that plays an important role in the proof of the lapse estimate appearing in Proposition 7.3.

Lemma 5.18

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta :=g^{ab}\nabla _a\nabla _b$$\end{document}Δ:=gab∇a∇b. Then for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0\le k \le N-3$$\end{document}0≤k≤N-3,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert [\Delta , \partial _{\textbf{u}}]V\Vert _{H^k}&\lesssim \Lambda (T) \Vert \partial _T V\Vert _{H^{k+1}} + \varepsilon e^{\max \{-1+\mu ,-\lambda \} T}\Vert V\Vert _{H^{k+2}}.\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖[Δ,∂u]V‖Hk≲Λ(T)‖∂TV‖Hk+1+εemax{-1+μ,-λ}T‖V‖Hk+2.

Proof

We compute\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} [\Delta , \partial _{\textbf{u}}]V_{ij}&= \Delta \hat{u}^0\cdot \partial _T V_{ij}+2\nabla ^a\hat{u}^0\nabla _a(\partial _T V_{ij}) - u^0 \partial _T g^{ab} \cdot \nabla _a\nabla _v V_{ij}\\&\quad + u^0 g^{ab}[\nabla _a\nabla _b, \partial _T]V_{ij} + \tau \big ( -\Delta u^c \cdot \hat{\nabla }_c V_{ij} - 2 \nabla _a u^c \nabla ^a \hat{\nabla }_c V_{ij}+ u^c\hat{\nabla }_c g^{ab}\\ &\quad \cdot \nabla _a \nabla _b V_{ij} \big ) + \tau u^c g^{ab} [\hat{\nabla }_c, \nabla _a \nabla _b]V_{ij}.\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}[Δ,∂u]Vij=Δu^0·∂TVij+2∇au^0∇a(∂TVij)-u0∂Tgab·∇a∇vVij+u0gab[∇a∇b,∂T]Vij+τ(-Δuc·∇^cVij-2∇auc∇a∇^cVij+uc∇^cgab·∇a∇bVij)+τucgab[∇^c,∇a∇b]Vij.Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$2\le k \le N-3$$\end{document}2≤k≤N-3. From Lemma 5.2 and (5.3) we find\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert [\Delta , \partial _{\textbf{u}}]V\Vert _{H^k}&\lesssim \Vert \nabla \hat{u}^0\Vert _{H^{k+1}}\Vert \partial _T V\Vert _{H^{k+1}} + \Vert \partial _T h \Vert _{H^{k}} \Vert V\Vert _{H^{k+2}} \\ &\quad + \Vert \partial _T \Gamma \Vert _{H^{k+1}}\Vert V\Vert _{H^{k+1}} + |\tau | \Vert u \Vert _{H^{k+2}}\Vert V\Vert _{H^{k+2}}.\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖[Δ,∂u]V‖Hk≲‖∇u^0‖Hk+1‖∂TV‖Hk+1+‖∂Th‖Hk‖V‖Hk+2+‖∂TΓ‖Hk+1‖V‖Hk+1+|τ|‖u‖Hk+2‖V‖Hk+2.The cases \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k=0,1$$\end{document}k=0,1 follow in the same way. The conclusion follows using Lemma 5.6, (4.2) and (5.2). \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Second Geometric Components Estimates

We now reach the final subsection of Sect. 5. The first lemma is an analogue of Lemma 4.5 for our top-order \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _{\textbf{u}}-$$\end{document}∂u-boosted geometric energy. The proof follows those in [1, Lemma 19] and [3, Lemma 7.2].

Lemma 5.19

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s\in \mathbb {Z}$$\end{document}s∈Z such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1\le s \le \ell $$\end{document}1≤s≤ℓ. There is a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta >0$$\end{document}δ>0 and a constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C>0$$\end{document}C>0 such that for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(g,\Sigma )\in B_{\delta }((\gamma ,0))$$\end{document}(g,Σ)∈Bδ((γ,0)) the inequality\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} (\partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^s h, \partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^s h)_{L^2_{g,\gamma }} + |(\partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^s v, \partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^{s-1}v)_{L^2_{g,\gamma }}|&\le C E^g_{\partial _{\textbf{u}}, 2\ell } \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(∂uLg,γsh,∂uLg,γsh)Lg,γ2+|(∂uLg,γsv,∂uLg,γs-1v)Lg,γ2|≤CE∂u,2ℓgholds. Furthermore \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E^g_{\partial _{\textbf{u}}, 2\ell } \ge 0$$\end{document}E∂u,2ℓg≥0.

Proof

Recall \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E^g_{\partial _{\textbf{u}}, 2\ell }$$\end{document}E∂u,2ℓg from Definition 3.11. We first note that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E^g_{\partial _{\textbf{u}}, 2\ell }|_{(h,v)=(\gamma ,0)} =0$$\end{document}E∂u,2ℓg|(h,v)=(γ,0)=0. Next, we see that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\gamma , 0)$$\end{document}(γ,0) is a critical point of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E^g_{\partial _{\textbf{u}}, 2\ell }$$\end{document}E∂u,2ℓg since the first derivative vanishes. Considering then the second derivative of this energy at \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\gamma ,0)$$\end{document}(γ,0), we see that the Hessian takes the form\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} D^2&(\mathcal {E}^g_{\partial _{\textbf{u}}, 2s}+c_E \Gamma ^g_{\partial _{\textbf{u}}, 2s})((h,k),(h,k)) \\&= 9 (\partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{\gamma ,\gamma }^s h, \partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{\gamma ,\gamma }^s h)_{L^2_{g,\gamma }}+ (\partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{\gamma ,\gamma }^s k, \partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{\gamma ,\gamma }^{s-1} k)_{L^2_{g,\gamma }}\\&\quad + c_E(\partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{\gamma ,\gamma }^{s-1} k, \partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{\gamma ,\gamma }^s h)_{L^2_{g,\gamma }}. \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}D2(E∂u,2sg+cEΓ∂u,2sg)((h,k),(h,k))=9(∂uLγ,γsh,∂uLγ,γsh)Lg,γ2+(∂uLγ,γsk,∂uLγ,γs-1k)Lg,γ2+cE(∂uLγ,γs-1k,∂uLγ,γsh)Lg,γ2.We claim that the Hessian is non-negative. By expanding in terms of the eigentensors of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {L}_{\gamma , \gamma }$$\end{document}Lγ,γ, we are left with terms of the type\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \lambda ^{2s-1} \Big ( 9 \lambda (\partial _{\textbf{u}}P_\lambda h, \partial _{\textbf{u}}P_\lambda h)_{L^2_{g,\gamma }}+ (\partial _{\textbf{u}}P_\lambda k, \partial _{\textbf{u}}P_\lambda k)_{L^2_{g,\gamma }} + c_E(\partial _{\textbf{u}}P_\lambda k, \partial _{\textbf{u}}P_\lambda h)_{L^2_{g,\gamma }}\Big ),\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}λ2s-1(9λ(∂uPλh,∂uPλh)Lg,γ2+(∂uPλk,∂uPλk)Lg,γ2+cE(∂uPλk,∂uPλh)Lg,γ2),where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P_\lambda $$\end{document}Pλ denotes the projection operator onto the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda $$\end{document}λ-eigenspace. The choice of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$c_E$$\end{document}cE ensures that the bracketed term is non-negative for the smallest eigenvalue \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda _0$$\end{document}λ0, which in turn implies non-negativity for all eigenvalues. Thus we find\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} D^2 (\mathcal {E}^g_{\partial _{\textbf{u}}, 2s}+c_E \Gamma ^g_{\partial _{\textbf{u}}, 2s})((h,k),(h,k))\ge 0.\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}D2(E∂u,2sg+cEΓ∂u,2sg)((h,k),(h,k))≥0.From this it follows that there is a constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C = C(\lambda _0, \gamma )>0$$\end{document}C=C(λ0,γ)>0 such that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \sum _{s=1}^\ell (\partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^s h, \partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^s h)_{L^2_{g,\gamma }} + |(\partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^s k, \partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^{s-1}k)_{L^2_{g,\gamma }}|&\le C E^g_{\partial _{\textbf{u}}, 2\ell }.\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}∑s=1ℓ(∂uLg,γsh,∂uLg,γsh)Lg,γ2+|(∂uLg,γsk,∂uLg,γs-1k)Lg,γ2|≤CE∂u,2ℓg.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

The next lemma provides estimates for the Sobolev norms of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _{\textbf{u}}h, \partial _{\textbf{u}}\Sigma $$\end{document}∂uh,∂uΣ in terms of the geometric energy \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E^g_{tot}$$\end{document}Etotg. This is natural given that we have constructed the functional \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E^g_{tot}$$\end{document}Etotg to precisely control such Sobolev norms.

Lemma 5.20

(Dust derivatives of geometric variables at high-regularity) We have,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert \partial _{\textbf{u}}h \Vert _{H^{N-1}}^2&\lesssim {E^g_{\partial _{\textbf{u}},N-1}}+\varepsilon ^2 e^{2\max \{-1+\mu ,-\lambda \} T}E^g_{N-1} + \Lambda (T)^4, \\ \Vert \partial _{\textbf{u}}\Sigma \Vert _{H^{N-2}}^2&\lesssim {E^g_{\partial _{\textbf{u}},N-1}}+ E^g_{N-1} + \varepsilon e^{\max \{-1+\mu ,-\lambda \} T}\\&\quad \times \big (\Vert \Sigma \Vert _{H^{N-1}}^2 + \Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^N}^2\big ) + \Lambda (T)^2.\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∂uh‖HN-12≲E∂u,N-1g+ε2e2max{-1+μ,-λ}TEN-1g+Λ(T)4,‖∂uΣ‖HN-22≲E∂u,N-1g+EN-1g+εemax{-1+μ,-λ}T×(‖Σ‖HN-12+‖g-γ‖HN2)+Λ(T)2.

Remark 5.21

Similar to Remark 5.17, we cannot apply Lemma 4.5 to the top-order Sobolev norms \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert \Sigma \Vert _{H^{N-1}}^2$$\end{document}‖Σ‖HN-12 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^{N}}^2$$\end{document}‖g-γ‖HN2. To estimate these terms by the geometric energy \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E^g_{tot}$$\end{document}Etotg we will instead need to use the auxiliary elliptic estimates of Sect. 6. It is also crucial in later analysis in Sect. 6 that these top-order norms above appear on the right hand side in Lemma 5.20 with a smallness factor of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon $$\end{document}ε.

Proof

Lemma 5.22

(Time derivative of geometric source terms) We have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert \partial _T F_h \Vert _{H^{N-2}} + \Vert \partial _T F_v \Vert _{H^{N-3}}&\lesssim E^g_{N-1}+ \Lambda (T).\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∂TFh‖HN-2+‖∂TFv‖HN-3≲EN-1g+Λ(T).

Proof

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$2\le k \le N-2$$\end{document}2≤k≤N-2. Taking a time derivative of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F_h$$\end{document}Fh as given in Definition 2.8 we see that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert \partial _T F_h\Vert _{H^k}&\lesssim \Vert \partial _T N \Vert _{H^k} + \big (\Vert \widehat{N}\Vert _{H^k} + \Vert X\Vert _{H^{k+1}} + \Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^{k+1}} \Vert X \Vert _{H^k} \big ) \Vert \partial _T h \Vert _{H^k} \\ &+\Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^k} \big ( \Vert \partial _T X \Vert _{H^{k+1}} + \Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^{k+1}}\Vert \partial _T X \Vert _{H^{k}}\big ).\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∂TFh‖Hk≲‖∂TN‖Hk+(‖N^‖Hk+‖X‖Hk+1+‖g-γ‖Hk+1‖X‖Hk)‖∂Th‖Hk+‖g-γ‖Hk(‖∂TX‖Hk+1+‖g-γ‖Hk+1‖∂TX‖Hk).The first estimate then follows by applying Lemma 5.6.

Next, let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$2\le k \le N-3$$\end{document}2≤k≤N-3. We take a time derivative of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F_v$$\end{document}Fv which gives\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \partial _T F_v&= \partial _T \nabla _i \nabla _j N + \partial _T N \cdot \big ( 2 \Sigma _{ic} \Sigma ^c_j -\tfrac{1}{3} g_{ij}- \Sigma _{ij} + \tau S_{ij}\big ) + 2 N \partial _T(\Sigma _{ic} \Sigma ^c_j)\\&\quad -\tfrac{1}{3}\widehat{N}\partial _T g_{ij} - \widehat{N}\partial _T \Sigma _{ij} - \tau N S_{ij} + \tau N \partial _T S_{ij}\\&\quad - \partial _T\big ( v_{im}\hat{\nabla }_j X^m + v_{mj}\hat{\nabla }_i X^m\big ).\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}∂TFv=∂T∇i∇jN+∂TN·(2ΣicΣjc-13gij-Σij+τSij)+2N∂T(ΣicΣjc)-13N^∂Tgij-N^∂TΣij-τNSij+τN∂TSij-∂T(vim∇^jXm+vmj∇^iXm).For the first term we use the commutator identity from (5.3) and recall that the lapse is a scalar. We obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert \partial _T F_v \Vert _{H^k}&\lesssim \Vert \partial _T N\Vert _{H^{k+2}} + \Vert [\partial _T, \nabla ]\nabla N\Vert _{H^k} + |\tau | \big ( \Vert S\Vert _{H^k} + \Vert \partial _T S\Vert _{H^k} \big ) \\ &\quad + \Vert \Sigma \Vert _{H^k}^2 + \Vert \partial _T \Sigma \Vert _{H^k}^2 + \Vert \partial _T g\Vert _{H^k}^2 + \Lambda (T)^2.\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∂TFv‖Hk≲‖∂TN‖Hk+2+‖[∂T,∇]∇N‖Hk+|τ|(‖S‖Hk+‖∂TS‖Hk)+‖Σ‖Hk2+‖∂TΣ‖Hk2+‖∂Tg‖Hk2+Λ(T)2.The conclusion then follows by Lemma 5.6 and the matter estimates in Lemma 4.4 and Lemma 5.12. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Corollary 5.23

(Dust derivative of geometric source terms) We have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert \partial _{\textbf{u}}F_h \Vert _{H^{N-2}} + \Vert \partial _{\textbf{u}}F_v\Vert _{H^{N-3}}&\lesssim E^g_{N-1} + \varepsilon e^{(-1+\mu )T}\big ( \Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^N}^2 + \Vert \Sigma \Vert _{H^{N-1}}^2 \big )+ \Lambda (T), \\ \Vert \partial _{\textbf{u}}F_h \Vert _{H^{2}}&\lesssim E^g_{N-1} + \Lambda (T).\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∂uFh‖HN-2+‖∂uFv‖HN-3≲EN-1g+εe(-1+μ)T(‖g-γ‖HN2+‖Σ‖HN-12)+Λ(T),‖∂uFh‖H2≲EN-1g+Λ(T).

Proof

The estimates follow by expanding out \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _{\textbf{u}}= u^0 \partial _T - \tau u^c \hat{\nabla }_c$$\end{document}∂u=u0∂T-τuc∇^c and using the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F_h, F_v$$\end{document}Fh,Fv estimates in Lemma 5.5 and the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _T F_h, \partial _T F_v$$\end{document}∂TFh,∂TFv estimates from Lemma 5.22. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Remark 5.24

Since we are dealing with geometric variables in the above corollary, and not matter variables, we roughly estimated the dust derivatives as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _{\textbf{u}}\sim \partial _T + \tau u^c *\nabla $$\end{document}∂u∼∂T+τuc∗∇. Note that doing so introduced the top-order Sobolev norms \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert \Sigma \Vert _{H^{N-1}}^2$$\end{document}‖Σ‖HN-12 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^{N}}^2$$\end{document}‖g-γ‖HN2. Crucially for later analysis in Corollary 6.4, however, is that these top-order norms appear with a coefficient of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon $$\end{document}ε.

Elliptic Estimate

In this section we prove an auxiliary elliptic estimate which allows us to control the top-order Sobolev norms of g and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Sigma $$\end{document}Σ in terms of the geometric energy functional \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E^g_{tot}$$\end{document}Etotg. Recall only the lower-order Sobolev norms are controlled using Lemma 4.5, and so a new idea is indeed needed to cover the top-order of regularity. We also remind the reader that the geometric energy \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E^g_{tot}$$\end{document}Etotg will eventually fulfill a strong decay estimate enabled by the inclusion of certain correction terms.

The main result of the section, Corollary 6.4, achieves the goal of the previous paragraph. To prove this corollary, we take the first-order equations of motion for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _T h, \partial _T \Sigma $$\end{document}∂Th,∂TΣ appearing in (2.8) and convert them into second-order equations involving a perturbed wave operator \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {W}$$\end{document}W and the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _{\textbf{u}}$$\end{document}∂u derivatives. We find, very schematically, that6.1\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }h \sim \mathcal {W}(h) + \partial _{\textbf{u}}\partial _T (h) + \tau u^a \hat{\nabla }_a\partial _{\textbf{u}}h. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Lg,γh∼W(h)+∂u∂T(h)+τua∇^a∂uh.By elliptic regularity for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }$$\end{document}Lg,γ, we can then prove an estimate on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^{N}} \sim \Vert \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }h \Vert _{H^{N-2}}$$\end{document}‖g-γ‖HN∼‖Lg,γh‖HN-2 by estimating the RHS of (6.1), see Proposition 6.3. A similar idea holds also for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Sigma $$\end{document}Σ. We note that this idea, albeit for a different gauge, was first introduced by Hadžić and Speck in [16].

Definition 6.1

(Operators \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {W},\mathcal {H}$$\end{document}W,H) Define the operators\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \mathcal {W}&:=\partial _T\partial _T-X^aX^b\hat{\nabla }_a \hat{\nabla }_b + N^2 \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }, \\ \mathcal {H}&:=N^2 \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }- X^aX^b\hat{\nabla }_a \hat{\nabla }_b - \tau ^2 \frac{u^a u^b}{(u^0)^2} \hat{\nabla }_b \hat{\nabla }_a ,\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}W:=∂T∂T-XaXb∇^a∇^b+N2Lg,γ,H:=N2Lg,γ-XaXb∇^a∇^b-τ2uaub(u0)2∇^b∇^a,which act on symmetric (0, 2)-tensors.

Due to the sign convention on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }$$\end{document}Lg,γ, see Definition 3.2, one can think of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {W}$$\end{document}W as being a kind of perturbed wave operator.

Lemma 6.2

(Wave equations for h, v) The differential Eq. (2.8) for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h=g-\gamma $$\end{document}h=g-γ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v = 6 \Sigma $$\end{document}v=6Σ imply\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {W}(h)= F_1, \qquad \mathcal {W}(v)= F_2, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}W(h)=F1,W(v)=F2,where,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert F_1 \Vert _{H^{N-2}}^2 + \Vert F_2 \Vert _{H^{N-3}}^2&\lesssim E^g_{N-1} + \varepsilon e^{-2\lambda T}\big (\Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^N}^2 + \Vert \Sigma \Vert _{H^{N-1}}^2\big ) + \Lambda (T)^2.\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖F1‖HN-22+‖F2‖HN-32≲EN-1g+εe-2λT(‖g-γ‖HN2+‖Σ‖HN-12)+Λ(T)2.

Proof

Rearranging (2.8) as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v = w^{-1}(\partial _T h+X^m\hat{\nabla }_m h-F_h)$$\end{document}v=w-1(∂Th+Xm∇^mh-Fh) and substituting this into (2.8) gives\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} -w^{-1}&(\partial _T w )v + w^{-1} (\partial _T^2 h +\partial _T(X^m\hat{\nabla }_m h)-\partial _T F_h)\\&= -2v - 9 w\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }h - X^m \hat{\nabla }_m v +6F_v.\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}-w-1(∂Tw)v+w-1(∂T2h+∂T(Xm∇^mh)-∂TFh)=-2v-9wLg,γh-Xm∇^mv+6Fv.Using again (2.8) we note that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \partial _T (X^a \hat{\nabla }_a h)&= -X^a X^b\hat{\nabla }_a\hat{\nabla }_b h -X^a\hat{\nabla }_a X^b \cdot \hat{\nabla }_b h+\partial _T X^a\cdot \hat{\nabla }_a h\\&\quad +X^m\hat{\nabla }_m (wv+F_h). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∂T(Xa∇^ah)=-XaXb∇^a∇^bh-Xa∇^aXb·∇^bh+∂TXa·∇^ah+Xm∇^m(wv+Fh).Rearranging terms (recall \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$9w^2 = N^2$$\end{document}9w2=N2) we find\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {W}(h) = F_1&:=X^a\hat{\nabla }_a X^b \cdot \hat{\nabla }_b h-\partial _T X^m\cdot \hat{\nabla }_m h -X^m\hat{\nabla }_m (wv+F_h)\\&\qquad +\partial _T F_h +v \partial _T w-2wv - To derive the equation for v we take the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs}
