Proposition 6.3

(Elliptic estimate using \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {W}, \partial _{\textbf{u}}$$\end{document}W,∂u operators) Let V be an arbitrary (0, 2)-tensor and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0\le k\le N-2$$\end{document}0≤k≤N-2. Then,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert V\Vert _{H^{k+2}}&\lesssim \Vert \mathcal {W}(V) \Vert _{H^{k}}+ \Vert \partial _{\textbf{u}}\partial _T (V) \Vert _{H^{k}} + |\tau | \Vert u^a \partial _{\textbf{u}}\hat{\nabla }_a(V) \Vert _{H^{k}}.\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖V‖Hk+2≲‖W(V)‖Hk+‖∂u∂T(V)‖Hk+|τ|‖ua∂u∇^a(V)‖Hk.

Proof

From (3.9) we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \partial _TV = (u^0)^{-1}\Big (\partial _{\textbf{u}}(V) + \tau u^a \hat{\nabla }_a V \Big ), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∂TV=(u0)-1(∂u(V)+τua∇^aV),and thus\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\partial _T\partial _TV = (u^0)^{-1}\Big (\partial _{\textbf{u}}( \partial _T V) + \tau u^a \hat{\nabla }_a \partial _T V\Big ), \\&\partial _T \hat{\nabla }_b V = (u^0)^{-1}\Big (\partial _{\textbf{u}}(\hat{\nabla }_b V) + \tau u^a \hat{\nabla }_a \hat{\nabla }_b V \Big ). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∂T∂TV=(u0)-1(∂u(∂TV)+τua∇^a∂TV),∂T∇^bV=(u0)-1(∂u(∇^bV)+τua∇^a∇^bV).Recalling \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\hat{u}^0= u^0 - 1/3$$\end{document}u^0=u0-1/3, we find6.2\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \mathcal {W}(V)&= \partial _T\partial _T V - X^aX^b\hat{\nabla }_a \hat{\nabla }_b V + N^2 \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }V \\&= (u^0)^{-1}\Big (\partial _{\textbf{u}}(\partial _T V) + \tau u^a \partial _T \hat{\nabla }_a V\Big ) - X^aX^b\hat{\nabla }_a \hat{\nabla }_b V + N^2 \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }V \\&= (u^0)^{-1}\partial _{\textbf{u}}(\partial _T V) + \frac{\tau u^a}{u^0} \frac{1}{u^0}\left( \partial _{\textbf{u}}(\hat{\nabla }_a V) + \tau u^b \hat{\nabla }_b \hat{\nabla }_a V \right) \\&\quad - X^aX^b\hat{\nabla }_a \hat{\nabla }_b V + N^2 \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }V \\&= (u^0)^{-1}\partial _{\textbf{u}}(\partial _T V) + \tau u^a (u^0)^{-2}\partial _{\textbf{u}}(\hat{\nabla }_a V) + \mathcal {H}(V).\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}W(V)=∂T∂TV-XaXb∇^a∇^bV+N2Lg,γV=(u0)-1(∂u(∂TV)+τua∂T∇^aV)-XaXb∇^a∇^bV+N2Lg,γV=(u0)-1∂u(∂TV)+τuau01u0∂u(∇^aV)+τub∇^b∇^aV-XaXb∇^a∇^bV+N2Lg,γV=(u0)-1∂u(∂TV)+τua(u0)-2∂u(∇^aV)+H(V).We view \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N^{-2}\mathcal {H}$$\end{document}N-2H as a perturbation off the elliptic operator \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }$$\end{document}Lg,γ. For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0\le k\le N-2$$\end{document}0≤k≤N-2,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert (N^{-2}\mathcal {H} - \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma })V\Vert _{H^k}&\lesssim \Vert X^aX^b\hat{\nabla }_a \hat{\nabla }_b V\Vert _{H^k} + \tau ^2 \big \Vert \frac{u^a u^b}{(u^0)^2} \hat{\nabla }_b \hat{\nabla }_a V\big \Vert _{H^k} \\ &\lesssim \varepsilon ^2 e^{(-2+2\mu )T}\big ( \Vert V\Vert _{H^{k+2}}+\Vert \Upsilon \Vert _{H^{k+1}}\Vert V\Vert _{H^{k+1}}\big ) \\ &\lesssim \varepsilon ^2 e^{(-2+2\mu )T}\Vert \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }V\Vert _{H^{k}}.\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖(N-2H-Lg,γ)V‖Hk≲‖XaXb∇^a∇^bV‖Hk+τ2‖uaub(u0)2∇^b∇^aV‖Hk≲ε2e(-2+2μ)T(‖V‖Hk+2+‖Υ‖Hk+1‖V‖Hk+1)≲ε2e(-2+2μ)T‖Lg,γV‖Hk.Suppose now \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {H}(V) =0$$\end{document}H(V)=0. By definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {H}$$\end{document}H, and elliptic regularity of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }$$\end{document}Lg,γ, this implies\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert N^2 \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }(V) \Vert _{L^2}&= \big \Vert X^aX^b\hat{\nabla }_a \hat{\nabla }_b V+\tau ^2 (u^0)^{-2}u^a u^b \hat{\nabla }_b \hat{\nabla }_a V\big \Vert _{L^2} \\ &\lesssim \varepsilon ^2 e^{2(-1+\mu )T} \Vert V\Vert _{H^2} \\ &\le C (C_1)^{-1} \varepsilon ^2 e^{2(-1+\mu )T} \Vert \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }V\Vert _{L^2},\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖N2Lg,γ(V)‖L2=‖XaXb∇^a∇^bV+τ2(u0)-2uaub∇^b∇^aV‖L2≲ε2e2(-1+μ)T‖V‖H2≤C(C1)-1ε2e2(-1+μ)T‖Lg,γV‖L2,for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C>0$$\end{document}C>0 some constant and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C_1>0$$\end{document}C1>0 as in Lemma 3.7. We also have a lower bound\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} C' \Vert \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }(V) \Vert _{L^2} \le \Vert N^2 \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }(V) \Vert _{L^2}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}C′‖Lg,γ(V)‖L2≤‖N2Lg,γ(V)‖L2.for another constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C'>0$$\end{document}C′>0. Choosing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon $$\end{document}ε sufficiently small so that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C (C_1)^{-1} \varepsilon ^2 e^{2(-1+\mu )T} < \varepsilon $$\end{document}C(C1)-1ε2e2(-1+μ)T<ε, we see these two inequalities imply\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} C' \Vert \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }(V) \Vert _{L^2} < \varepsilon \Vert \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }(V) \Vert _{L^2} . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}C′‖Lg,γ(V)‖L2<ε‖Lg,γ(V)‖L2.For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon $$\end{document}ε sufficiently small this implies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }(V)\Vert _{L^2} = 0$$\end{document}‖Lg,γ(V)‖L2=0 and so \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$V \in \ker \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }$$\end{document}V∈kerLg,γ. However, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ker {\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }}=0$$\end{document}kerLg,γ=0, and so for small data \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {H}$$\end{document}H also has trivial kernel and thus we obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert V\Vert _{H^{k+2}} \lesssim \Vert N^{-2} \mathcal {H}(V)\Vert _{H^k} \lesssim \Vert V\Vert _{H^{k+2}} . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖V‖Hk+2≲‖N-2H(V)‖Hk≲‖V‖Hk+2.Putting this together with (6.2) we find\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert V\Vert _{H^{k+2}}&\lesssim \Vert N^{-2} \mathcal {W}(V) \Vert _{H^{k}}+ \Vert (u^0)^{-1}N^{-2} \partial _{\textbf{u}}\partial _T (V) \Vert _{H^{k}}\\&\quad + |\tau | \Vert (u^0)^{-2}u^a N^{-2} \partial _{\textbf{u}}\hat{\nabla }_a (V) \Vert _{H^{k}}.\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖V‖Hk+2≲‖N-2W(V)‖Hk+‖(u0)-1N-2∂u∂T(V)‖Hk+|τ|‖(u0)-2uaN-2∂u∇^a(V)‖Hk.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

We can now bring together the previous results and estimate the top-order Sobolev norms of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g, \Sigma $$\end{document}g,Σ in terms of our geometric energy functionals \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${E^g_{\partial _{\textbf{u}},N-1}}$$\end{document}E∂u,N-1g and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E^g_{N-1}$$\end{document}EN-1g.

Corollary 6.4

We have,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^N}^2 +\Vert \Sigma \Vert _{H^{N-1}}^2&\lesssim {E^g_{\partial _{\textbf{u}},N-1}}+ E^g_{N-1} + \Lambda (T)^2.\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖g-γ‖HN2+‖Σ‖HN-12≲E∂u,N-1g+EN-1g+Λ(T)2.

Proof

Corollary 6.5

For I a multi-index,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned}&\sum _{|I|\le N-1}\Vert \partial _{\textbf{u}}\nabla ^I X\Vert _{L^2}+ \Vert \partial _{\textbf{u}}\nabla ^I \widehat{N}\Vert _{L^2} \lesssim \Lambda (T), \\&\sum _{|I|\le N-1}\Vert \partial _{\textbf{u}}\nabla ^I (g-\gamma )\Vert _{L^2} + \sum _{|I|\le N-2}\Vert \partial _{\textbf{u}}\nabla ^I \Sigma \Vert _{L^2}\\&\quad \lesssim ({E^g_{\partial _{\textbf{u}},N-1}})^{1/2} + (E^g_{N-1})^{1/2}+ \Lambda (T).\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}∑|I|≤N-1‖∂u∇IX‖L2+‖∂u∇IN^‖L2≲Λ(T),∑|I|≤N-1‖∂u∇I(g-γ)‖L2+∑|I|≤N-2‖∂u∇IΣ‖L2≲(E∂u,N-1g)1/2+(EN-1g)1/2+Λ(T).The same estimates hold with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nabla $$\end{document}∇ replaced by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\hat{\nabla }$$\end{document}∇^.

Proof

By Lemma 5.20 and Corollary 6.4\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert \partial _{\textbf{u}}h \Vert _{H^{N-1}} + \Vert \partial _{\textbf{u}}\Sigma \Vert _{H^{N-2}}&\lesssim ({E^g_{\partial _{\textbf{u}},N-1}})^{1/2} + (E^g_{N-1})^{1/2} + \Lambda (T).\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∂uh‖HN-1+‖∂uΣ‖HN-2≲(E∂u,N-1g)1/2+(EN-1g)1/2+Λ(T).Next, let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nabla ^{I} = \nabla _{a_1} \ldots \nabla _{a_{k}}$$\end{document}∇I=∇a1…∇ak where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k:=|I|\le N-1$$\end{document}k:=|I|≤N-1 and let V be a (0, 2)-tensor on M. Then, by (5.1), (5.2) and Lemma 5.13,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert [ \partial _{\textbf{u}}, \nabla ^I] V \Vert _{L^2}&\lesssim \Vert [ \partial _{\textbf{u}}, \nabla _{a_1}] \nabla _{a_2} \ldots \nabla _{a_k} V \Vert _{L^2} + \cdots + \Vert [ \partial _{\textbf{u}}, \nabla ] V \Vert _{H^{k-1}} \\ &\lesssim \varepsilon e^{\max \{-1+\mu ,-\lambda \}T} \Vert V\Vert _{H^{k}} + \Lambda (T)\Vert \partial _T V\Vert _{H^{k-1}}\\&\quad + \Lambda (T) \Vert \partial _T \Gamma (g) \Vert _{H^{N-3}} \Vert V \Vert _{H^{k-2}}. \end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖[∂u,∇I]V‖L2≲‖[∂u,∇a1]∇a2…∇akV‖L2+⋯+‖[∂u,∇]V‖Hk-1≲εemax{-1+μ,-λ}T‖V‖Hk+Λ(T)‖∂TV‖Hk-1+Λ(T)‖∂TΓ(g)‖HN-3‖V‖Hk-2.This implies\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert \partial _{\textbf{u}}\nabla ^I V \Vert _{L^2} \lesssim \Vert \partial _{\textbf{u}}V \Vert _{H^k} + \varepsilon e^{\max \{-1+\mu ,-\lambda \} T}\Vert V\Vert _{H^{k}} + \Lambda (T)\Vert \partial _T V\Vert _{H^{k-1}} .\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∂u∇IV‖L2≲‖∂uV‖Hk+εemax{-1+μ,-λ}T‖V‖Hk+Λ(T)‖∂TV‖Hk-1.A simple check shows that when considering the appropriate commutator expression (see (5.10)) on the scalar lapse we will pick up \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} not \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert N \Vert _{H^k}$$\end{document}‖N‖Hk. The result then follow, using Lemma 5.4 and Lemma 5.6. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Lemma 6.6

Let V be a (0, 2)-tensor on M and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p\in \{1,2\}$$\end{document}p∈{1,2}. Then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert \partial _{\textbf{u}}V\Vert _{H^p}&\lesssim \sum _{|I|\le p} \Vert \partial _{\textbf{u}}\nabla ^I V \Vert _{L^2} + \varepsilon e^{\max \{-1+\mu ,-\lambda \} T}\Vert V\Vert _{H^{1}} +\Lambda (T)\Vert \partial _T V\Vert _{H^{p-1}}.\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∂uV‖Hp≲∑|I|≤p‖∂u∇IV‖L2+εemax{-1+μ,-λ}T‖V‖H1+Λ(T)‖∂TV‖Hp-1.

Proof

A straight forward application of commutator identities. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Lapse and Shift Estimates

In this section we first establish the basic estimates on the lapse, shift and their time derivatives using elliptic estimates and general formula presented in [1]. The most exciting results lie in the novel top-order lapse and shift estimates given in Sect. 7.1.

Lemma 7.1

For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$3\le k \le N$$\end{document}3≤k≤N,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \Vert \widehat{N}\Vert _{H^k}&\lesssim |\tau | \Vert \rho \Vert _{H^{k-2}} + \varepsilon ^2 e^{-2\lambda T},\\ \Vert X \Vert _{H^k}&\lesssim |\tau | \Vert \rho \Vert _{H^{k-3}} + \varepsilon ^2 e^{\max \{-2\lambda , -2+\mu \}T}.\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖N^‖Hk≲|τ|‖ρ‖Hk-2+ε2e-2λT,‖X‖Hk≲|τ|‖ρ‖Hk-3+ε2emax{-2λ,-2+μ}T.

Proof

Recall from (2.7b) the lapse equation of motion\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} (\Delta - \tfrac{1}{3})N = N(|\Sigma |_g^2 + \tau \eta ) - 1. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(Δ-13)N=N(|Σ|g2+τη)-1.By elliptic regularity for the standard Laplacian \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta $$\end{document}Δ this implies\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert \widehat{N}\Vert _{H^k}&\le C \big ( \Vert \Sigma \Vert ^2_{H^{k-2}} + |\tau | \Vert \eta \Vert _{H^{k-2}} \big ),\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖N^‖Hk≤C(‖Σ‖k-22+|τ|‖η‖Hk-2),and the desired result follows by Lemma 4.4.

Similarly, from the shift equation of motion (2.7b), we find that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert X \Vert _{H^k}&\le C \big ( \Vert \Sigma \Vert _{H^{k-2}}^2 + \Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^{k-1}}^2 + |\tau | \Vert \eta \Vert _{H^{k-3}} + \tau ^2 \Vert N \jmath \Vert _{H^{k-2}} \big ),\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖X‖Hk≤C(‖Σ‖Hk-22+‖g-γ‖Hk-12+|τ|‖η‖Hk-3+τ2‖Nȷ‖Hk-2),and the desired result follows by Lemma 4.4. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Lemma 7.2

For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$4\le k \le N-1$$\end{document}4≤k≤N-1,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert \partial _T N \Vert _{H^k} + \Vert \partial _T X \Vert _{H^k}&\lesssim \Vert \widehat{N}\Vert _{H^k}+\Vert X\Vert _{H^k} + |\tau |\Vert \rho \Vert _{H^{k-2}} + \varepsilon ^2 e^{\max \{-2\lambda , -2+\mu \}T}.\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∂TN‖Hk+‖∂TX‖Hk≲‖N^‖Hk+‖X‖Hk+|τ|‖ρ‖Hk-2+ε2emax{-2λ,-2+μ}T.

Proof

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$4\le k \le N-1$$\end{document}4≤k≤N-1. Following [1], we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert \partial _T N \Vert _{H^k}&\lesssim \Vert \widehat{N}\Vert _{H^k}+\Vert X\Vert _{H^k} + \Vert \Sigma \Vert _{H^{k-1}}^2 + \Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^k}^2\\&\quad + |\tau | \big (\Vert S \Vert _{H^{k-2}} + \Vert \eta \Vert _{H^{k-2}}+\Vert \partial _T \eta \Vert _{H^{k-2}}\big ) \\ &\lesssim \Vert \widehat{N}\Vert _{H^k}+\Vert X\Vert _{H^k} + |\tau |\Vert \rho \Vert _{H^{k-2}}+ \varepsilon ^2 e^{\max \{-2\lambda , -2+\mu \}T},\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∂TN‖Hk≲‖N^‖Hk+‖X‖Hk+‖Σ‖Hk-12+‖g-γ‖Hk2+|τ|(‖S‖Hk-2+‖η‖Hk-2+‖∂Tη‖Hk-2)≲‖N^‖Hk+‖X‖Hk+|τ|‖ρ‖Hk-2+ε2emax{-2λ,-2+μ}T,where we used Lemma 4.4 and Lemma 5.12.

Again, using a general expression given in [1, §7], we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert \partial _T X \Vert _{H^k}&\lesssim \Vert \widehat{N}\Vert _{H^k}+ \Vert X\Vert _{H^{k}}+ \Vert \Sigma \Vert _{H^{k}}^2 +\Vert g-\gamma \Vert _{H^k} ^2 \\&\quad + \tau \big (\Vert S \Vert _{H^{k-2}} + \Vert \eta \Vert _{H^{k-2}}+\Vert \partial _T \eta \Vert _{H^{k-2}}\big )+ \tau ^2 \Vert \jmath \Vert _{H^{k-1}} + \tau ^3 \Vert \underline{T} \Vert _{H^{k-1}} \\&\lesssim \Vert \widehat{N}\Vert _{H^k}+ \Vert X\Vert _{H^{k}} + |\tau | \Vert \rho \Vert _{H^{k-2}} +\varepsilon ^2 e^{\max \{-2\lambda , -2+\mu \}T},\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∂TX‖Hk≲‖N^‖Hk+‖X‖Hk+‖Σ‖Hk2+‖g-γ‖Hk2+τ(‖S‖Hk-2+‖η‖Hk-2+‖∂Tη‖Hk-2)+τ2‖ȷ‖Hk-1+τ3‖T_‖Hk-1≲‖N^‖Hk+‖X‖Hk+|τ|‖ρ‖Hk-2+ε2emax{-2λ,-2+μ}T,where we again used Lemma 4.4 and Lemma 5.12. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Additional Top-order Lapse and Shift Estimates

In this section we establish important auxiliary estimates in two propositions for the lapse and shift variables. The first proposition is used to estimate \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _{\textbf{u}}\nabla _i \nabla _j N$$\end{document}∂u∇i∇jN. This somewhat unusual expression comes from a dust derivative acting on the first term in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F_v$$\end{document}Fv (see Definition 2.8), and appears later on in the energy estimates for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E^g_{tot}$$\end{document}Etotg.

Proposition 7.3

(Top-order auxiliary lapse estimate) We have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \big (\Delta -\tfrac{1}{3}\big )\partial _{\textbf{u}}\nabla _i \nabla _j N =\mathcal {F}_{N,\textbf{u}} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(Δ-13)∂u∇i∇jN=FN,uwhere,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert \mathcal {F}_{N,\textbf{u}}\Vert _{H^{N-4}} \lesssim \Lambda (T) + {E^g_{\partial _{\textbf{u}},N-1}}+ E^g_{N-1}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖FN,u‖HN-4≲Λ(T)+E∂u,N-1g+EN-1g.

Proof

Start by commuting the lapse Eq. (2.7b) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _{\textbf{u}}\nabla _i \nabla _j$$\end{document}∂u∇i∇j:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \big (\Delta -\tfrac{1}{3}\big )\partial _{\textbf{u}}\nabla _i \nabla _j N&= [\Delta ,\partial _{\textbf{u}}]\nabla _i\nabla _j N + \partial _{\textbf{u}}[\Delta , \nabla _i\nabla _j]N + \partial _{\textbf{u}}\nabla _i \nabla _j \Big ( N(|\Sigma |_g^2 - \tau \eta )\Big )\\&=: L_1 + L_2 +L_3=: \mathcal {F}_{N,\textbf{u}}.\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(Δ-13)∂u∇i∇jN=[Δ,∂u]∇i∇jN+∂u[Δ,∇i∇j]N+∂u∇i∇j(N(|Σ|g2-τη))=:L1+L2+L3=:FN,u.We investigate each of the terms in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {F}_{N,\textbf{u}}$$\end{document}FN,u separately. The first term \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L_1 $$\end{document}L1 we estimate using (5.2), (5.3) and Lemma 5.18\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert L_1\Vert _{H^{N-4}}&\lesssim \Lambda (T)\Vert \partial _T \nabla _i\nabla _j N\Vert _{H^{N-3}} + \varepsilon e^{\max \{-1+\mu ,-\lambda \} T}\Vert \nabla _i\nabla _j N\Vert _{H^{N-2}} \\ &\lesssim \Lambda (T)\Big ( \Vert \partial _T N\Vert _{H^{N-1}} + \Vert [\partial _T, \nabla _i]\nabla _j N\Vert _{H^{N-3}} \Big ) \\ &\quad + \varepsilon e^{\max \{-1+\mu ,-\lambda \} T}\Vert \widehat{N}\Vert _{H^{N}} \\ &\lesssim \Lambda (T)^2 + \varepsilon e^{\max \{-1+\mu ,-\lambda \} T}\Lambda (T).\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖L1‖HN-4≲Λ(T)‖∂T∇i∇jN‖HN-3+εemax{-1+μ,-λ}T‖∇i∇jN‖HN-2≲Λ(T)(‖∂TN‖HN-1+‖[∂T,∇i]∇jN‖HN-3)+εemax{-1+μ,-λ}T‖N^‖HN≲Λ(T)2+εemax{-1+μ,-λ}TΛ(T).For the next term, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L_2$$\end{document}L2, we first compute, for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi $$\end{document}ϕ a scalar,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} Remark 7.4

In our energy estimates later on we need to estimate a term of the type \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _{\textbf{u}}\hat{\nabla }^{N-2} F_v$$\end{document}∂u∇^N-2Fv where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\hat{\nabla }^{N-2}$$\end{document}∇^N-2 indicates \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N-2$$\end{document}N-2 covariant derivatives and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F_v$$\end{document}Fv is given in Definition 2.8. We would like to commute the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _{\textbf{u}}$$\end{document}∂u operator past these covariant derivatives. However, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F_v$$\end{document}Fv contains a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nabla \nabla N$$\end{document}∇∇N term, and we cannot commute \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _{\textbf{u}}$$\end{document}∂u past \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N-2$$\end{document}N-2 copies of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nabla $$\end{document}∇ as well as the extra two derivatives in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nabla \nabla N$$\end{document}∇∇N since we only control \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^a$$\end{document}ua in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$H^{N-1}$$\end{document}HN-1. The previous proposition crucially allows us to avoid this issue. Note also that by commuting in the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _{\textbf{u}}$$\end{document}∂u operator, instead of doing the rough expansion \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _{\textbf{u}}\sim \partial _T + \tau u^c *\nabla $$\end{document}∂u∼∂T+τuc∗∇, we gain an additional derivative in Corollary 7.5 compared to Corollary 5.23.

Corollary 7.5

For I a multi-index of order \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|I|\le N-2$$\end{document}|I|≤N-2,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \Vert \partial _{\textbf{u}}\hat{\nabla }^I F_v\Vert _{L^2}&\lesssim \Lambda (T) + {E^g_{\partial _{\textbf{u}},N-1}}+E^g_{N-1}.\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∂u∇^IFv‖L2≲Λ(T)+E∂u,N-1g+EN-1g.

Proof

Write \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\hat{\nabla }^{I} = \hat{\nabla }_{a_1} \ldots \hat{\nabla }_{a_{k}}$$\end{document}∇^I=∇^a1…∇^ak where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k:=|I|\le N-2$$\end{document}k:=|I|≤N-2. Using the definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F_v$$\end{document}Fv in Definition 2.8 and the estimates in Corollary 6.5, we see the most subtle terms (from the point of view of regularity) are \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nabla _i\nabla _j N$$\end{document}∇i∇jN and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N\tau S_{ij}$$\end{document}NτSij. For these terms we look at what happens when we commute in the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _{\textbf{u}}$$\end{document}∂u operator using Lemma 5.13:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned}&\Vert [\partial _{\textbf{u}}, \hat{\nabla }^I] \nabla _i\nabla _j N \Vert _{L^2} \\&\quad \lesssim \Vert [ \partial _{\textbf{u}}, \hat{\nabla In our energy estimates later on we need to estimate a term of the type \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _{\textbf{u}}\hat{\nabla }^{N-1} F_h$$\end{document}∂u∇^N-1Fh. Similar to the issue discussed in Remark 7.4, the problematic term here is the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {L}_X g$$\end{document}LXg term appearing in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F_h$$\end{document}Fh (see Definition 2.8). The second proposition of this section estimates this problematic term. Since, however, the shift is not scalar-valued like the lapse, a replication of the ideas used in Proposition 7.3 ends up failing. Instead, our proof involves a remarkable combination of commutator estimates, the Bianchi identity and the Einstein equations in the CMCSH gauge.

Proposition 7.6

(Top-order estimate for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {L}_X g$$\end{document}LXg) We have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\Big | \big (\partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^{\ell -1}(\Delta \mathscr {L}_X g),\partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^{\ell }(h)\big )_{L^2_{g,\gamma }} \Big | \\&\quad \lesssim \Lambda (T) {E^g_{\partial _{\textbf{u}},N-1}}+ \Lambda (T) E^g_{N-1} + \Lambda (T) ({E^g_{\partial _{\textbf{u}},N-1}})^{1/2}.\end{aligned}$$\end{document}|(∂uLg,γℓ-1(ΔLXg),∂uLg,γℓ(h))Lg,γ2|≲Λ(T)E∂u,N-1g+Λ(T)EN-1g+Λ(T)(E∂u,N-1g)1/2.

Proof

Recall \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$2(\ell -1) = N-3$$\end{document}2(ℓ-1)=N-3. \text {Ric}^c _a X_c + \mathcal {F}^X_a. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ΔXa=-RicacXc+FaX.By contracting the Bianchi identity, one finds that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \nabla ^a\text {Riem}_{abcd} = \nabla _c \text {Ric}_{bd} - \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Geometric Energy Estimates

In this section we establish energy estimates for the geometric energy functionals. We first prove estimates for the time-derivatives of the lower-order energy functional \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${E^g}_{N-1}$$\end{document}EgN-1, and then for the top-order energy functional \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {E}^g_{\partial _{\textbf{u}},N-1}$$\end{document}E∂u,N-1g. Recall \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha , c_E$$\end{document}α,cE and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E^g_{tot}$$\end{document}Etotg are given in Definitions 3.8 and 3.10.

Proposition 8.1

There exists a constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C>0$$\end{document}C>0 such that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \partial _T {E^g}_{N-1} \le - 2 \alpha {E^g}_{N-1} + C \Lambda (T) ({E^g}_{N-1})^{1/2} + C({E^g}_{N-1})^{3/2}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∂TEgN-1≤-2αEgN-1+CΛ(T)(EgN-1)1/2+C(EgN-1)3/2.

Proof

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le k \le N-1$$\end{document}0≤k≤N-1. Using an estimate from [1, Lemma 20] together with Lemma 4.4, we find\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \partial _T {E^g}_k&\le - 2 \alpha {E^g}_k + 6 ({E^g}_k)^{1/2} |\tau | \Vert N S \Vert _{H^{k-1}} + C({E^g}_k)^{3/2}\\&\quad + C({E^g}_k)^{1/2} \big ( |\tau | \Vert \eta \Vert _{H^{k-1}} + \tau ^2 \Vert N j \Vert _{H^{k-2}} \big ) \\ &\le - 2 \alpha {E^g}_k + C({E^g}_k)^{1/2} |\tau | \Vert \rho \Vert _{H^{k-1}} + C({E^g}_k)^{3/2} + C \Lambda (T) ({E^g}_k)^{1/2} .\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}∂TEgk≤-2αEgk+6(Egk)1/2|τ|‖NS‖Hk-1+C(Egk)3/2+C(Egk)1/2(|τ|‖η‖Hk-1+τ2‖Nj‖Hk-2)≤-2αEgk+C(Egk)1/2|τ|‖ρ‖Hk-1+C(Egk)3/2+CΛ(T)(Egk)1/2.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

We now turn to the time evolution of the top-order \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _{\textbf{u}}-$$\end{document}∂u-boosted geometric energy. The main result is stated in Theorem 8.3. For ease of presentation however, the proof relies on the subsequent estimates given in Propositions 8.4, 8.5, 8.7, 8.8, and 8.10.

Remark 8.2

The key auxiliary estimates of the previous section, Corollary 7.5 and Proposition 7.6, are applied in Proposition 8.5 and Proposition 8.4 respectively. The important integration by parts identity (5.6c) in Lemma 5.3, where one term is brought back onto the LHS, is applied in Proposition 8.8 (see (8.10)).

Theorem 8.3

There exists a constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C>0$$\end{document}C>0 such that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \partial _T \mathcal {E}^g_{\partial _{\textbf{u}},N-1}&\le -2\alpha \mathcal {E}^g_{\partial _{\textbf{u}},N-1} + C\big ( |\tau | \Vert u\Vert _{H^{N-1}}+ \Lambda (T) \big )E^g_{tot}+ C\Lambda (T) (E^g_{tot})^{1/2} \\ &\quad + C(E^g_{tot})^{3/2} + C\Lambda (T)^2.\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∂TE∂u,N-1g≤-2αE∂u,N-1g+C(|τ|‖u‖HN-1+Λ(T))Etotg+CΛ(T)(Etotg)1/2+C(Etotg)3/2+CΛ(T)2.

Recalling Definition 3.11, the energy \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${E^g_{\partial _{\textbf{u}},N-1}}$$\end{document}E∂u,N-1g consists of a sum over lower-order energies. The top-order is the most subtle, so we focus on this and merely remark that the estimates for the lower-orders follow in the same (or possibly easier) way. The top-order energy consists of two parts, an \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {E}^g_{\partial _{\textbf{u}},N-1}$$\end{document}E∂u,N-1g term and a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$c_E \Gamma ^g_{\partial _{\textbf{u}},N-1}$$\end{document}cEΓ∂u,N-1g term. We treat these separately for the moment.

Remark 8.6

In the next proposition, it is crucial that the three terms of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_3$$\end{document}G3 are treated together, since there is an important cancellation that appears.

Proposition 8.7

We have,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} |G_3|+|G_5|+|G_6|&\lesssim \big ( |\tau | \Vert u\Vert _{H^{N-1}}+ \Lambda (T) \big )E^g_{tot}+ \Lambda (T) ({E^g_{\partial _{\textbf{u}},N-1}})^{1/2} \\ &\quad +(E^g_{tot})^{3/2}+ \Lambda (T)^2.\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}|G3|+|G5|+|G6|≲(|τ|‖u‖HN-1+Λ(T))Etotg+Λ(T)(E∂u,N-1g)1/2+(Etotg)3/2+Λ(T)2.

Proof

First note from (3.1) that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \big (\partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^{\ell }(w \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }h),\partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^{\ell -1}(v)\big )_{L^2_{g,\gamma }}&= \big (\partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^{\ell -1}(w \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }h),\partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^{\ell }(v)\big )_{L^2_{g,\gamma }} + G_3^e ,\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}(∂uLg,γℓ(wLg,γh),∂uLg,γℓ-1(v))Lg,γ2=(∂uLg,γℓ-1(wLg,γh),∂uLg,γℓ(v))Lg,γ2+G3e,where we have defined\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&G_3^e :=\big ([\partial _{\textbf{u}},\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }]\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^{\ell -1}(w \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }h),\partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^{\ell -1}(v)\big )_{L^2_{g,\gamma }}\\&\quad +\big (\partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^{\ell -1}(w \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }h),[\partial _{\textbf{u}},\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }]\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma gives,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \tfrac{1}{9} G_3&= \big (\partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^{\ell }(wv),\partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^{\ell }(h)\big )_{L^2_{g,\gamma }} -\big (\partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^{\ell -1}(w \mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }h),\partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^{\ell Proposition 8.8

In this final part of the section we prove estimates on the commutator error terms \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_c$$\end{document}Gc and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_{cc}$$\end{document}Gcc. We first prove a preliminary lemma concerning the commutator between the operators \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _T$$\end{document}∂T and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }$$\end{document}∂uLg,γ, and then use this lemma in the subsequent proposition.

Lemma 8.9

We have,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \Vert [\partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^{\ell },\partial _T]h \Vert _{L^2} + \Vert [\partial _{\textbf{u}}\mathcal {L}_{g, \gamma }^{\ell -1},\partial _T]\Sigma \Vert _{H^1}&\lesssim {E^g_{\partial _{\textbf{u}},N-1}}+ E^g_{N-1} + \Lambda (T).\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖[∂uLg,γℓ,∂T]h‖L2+‖[∂uLg,γℓ-1,∂T]Σ‖H1≲E∂u,N-1g+EN-1g+Λ(T).

We have,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} |G_{c}| + |G_{cc}| \lesssim (E^g_{tot})^{3/2}+ \Lambda (T) ({E^g_{\partial _{\textbf{u}},N-1}})^{1/2}.\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}|Gc|+|Gcc|≲(Etotg)3/2+Λ(T)(E∂u,N-1g)1/2.

Energy Estimates for the Dust Variables

In this section we establish energy estimates for the dust variables \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho $$\end{document}ρ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^a$$\end{document}ua in two propositions. An integration by parts identity, where due to symmetry a high-derivative term can be brought back onto the LHS, plays a crucial role in both propositions. We write the argument out explicitly for the first proposition, although note in principle the argument is just as in Lemma 5.3, (5.6c).

Definition 9.1

[\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {E}_k{[}\rho {]}$$\end{document}Ek[ρ]] Define the functionals\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} E_k[\rho ](T)&:=\frac{1}{2} \int _M |\nabla ^k \rho (T, \cdot )|^2 \mu _g, \qquad \mathcal {E}_k[\rho ](T)&:=\sum _{0\le \ell \le k} E_\ell [\rho ](T).\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}Ek[ρ](T):=12∫M|∇kρ(T,·)|2μg,Ek[ρ](T):=∑0≤ℓ≤kEℓ[ρ](T).

Proposition 9.2

[Time evolution of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {E}_{N-2}{[}\rho {]}$$\end{document}EN-2[ρ] for fluid energy density] We have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} |\partial _T \mathcal {E}_{N-2}[\rho ](T)| \lesssim \varepsilon e^{\max \{-1+\mu ,-\lambda \} T} \mathcal {E}_{N-2}[\rho ](T). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}|∂TEN-2[ρ](T)|≲εemax{-1+μ,-λ}TEN-2[ρ](T).

Proof

Recall from (2.7d) that the equation of motion for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho $$\end{document}ρ is\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \partial _T \rho&= (u^0)^{-1}\rho F_\rho + \tau (u^0)^{-1} u^a \nabla _a \rho .\end{aligned}\end{aligned}$$\end{document}∂Tρ=(u0)-1ρFρ+τ(u0)-1ua∇aρ.Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$3 \le k \le N-2$$\end{document}3≤k≤N-2. Using (3.2) and the integration by parts estimate (5.6b), we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\begin{aligned} \partial _T E_k[\rho ](T)&\lesssim \Vert \widehat{N}\Vert _\infty E_k[\rho ](T) + \int _M \partial _T (|\nabla ^k \rho |^2)\mu _g x+ \int _M X^c \hat{\nabla }_c (|\nabla ^k \rho |^2) \mu _g \\&\lesssim \Big ( \Vert \widehat{N}\Vert _{H^2} + \Vert X\Vert _{H^3} \Big ) E_k[\rho ](T)\\&\quad +\int _M \partial _T \Big ( g^{a_1 b_1} \cdots g^{a_k b_k}\Big ) (\nabla ^{a_1}\cdots \nabla ^{a_k}\rho )(\nabla _{a_1}\cdots \nabla _{a_k}\rho )\mu _g \\&\quad + \int _M g^{a_1 Proof of Theorem 1.2

In this final section we bring together our main estimates to conclude the bootstrap argument. One standard, yet technical, aspect of the argument is deferred to Appendix 11.

Proof

Smallness of the initial data guarantees the existence of a constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb}
